PERFORMANCE TEST 
Performance testing shall be carried out after functional testing and with the rated load applied. The 
object of performance testing is to determine whether the equipment performs to the manufacturer’s 
specification. This should include the operation of all controls to determine whether the equipment 
operates correctly and smoothly at the rated speeds; and is free from wear and other damage. 
Performance testing shall be carried out on all functions of the forklift including brakes and safety devices. 
 LOAD TEST 
Before and after any load testing the equipment shall be thoroughly examined. The objective of load 
testing is to determine whether the equipment is stable, structurally sound and fit for the use for which it 
was designed. The test program drawn up by the competent person should include every load-bearing 
part. The competent person should determine whether the original or if appropriate the previous test 
certificate is satisfactory, and subsequent testing should be based on this with any necessary 
modifications to suit the requirements of the particular item of equipment. 
Note: The competent person might wish to consult the manufacturer or other design authority to ensure 
that the selected test program is adequate. 
 
 FORKLIFT HYDRAULIC MAXIMUM ACCEPTABLE SLIPPAGE (CREEP) 
The descent of the rated load caused by a leakage in the hydraulic system shall not exceed 100 mm 
during the first 10 minutes, with the oil in the hydraulic system at normal working temperature. The 
leakages referred to those occurring under normal conditions of use of the forklift trucks; external 
leakage's resulting from abnormal conditions of use or faulty constructions are not taken into 
consideration. 
The leakage rate of the complete hydraulic tilting system shall allow only an average pivoting speed of 
the mast to the front, of less than five degrees in the first 10 minutes, from the vertical mast position when 
the truck is raising its rated load at a height of 2.5 meters, or in the case of trucks which are less than 2.5 
meters, at their maximum height. 
The average pivoting speed allowed by leakages shall not exceed 0.5 degrees in one minute for truck 
with a maximum tilt of less than 5.0 degrees. 
The above mentioned Forklift Truck Specifications have been adapted from the EEC Directive 86/663. 
 
 FORKLIFT AND OTHER EQUIPMENT WHICH HAVE BEEN ALTERED OR REPAIRED 
In the event of any alteration or repair that could affect the stability of the forklift, stability testing shall be 
carried out in accordance with the original forklift specification. In the event of any alteration or repair 
which affects the strength of the forklift, proof load testing shall be carried out to ensure that all parts 
affected by the repair are subjected to the test loads in accordance with the original forklift specification. 
Note: It might be necessary to confirm with the manufacturer or other design authority the loads to be 
applied following such alterations or repairs. 
 
 PROOF LOAD TEST CRITERIA  
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12 
Third Party Inspection Company to advice the required Action Plan for the proof load test and to be sent 
to ADNOC Group for review and approval prior to mobilize the Third Party Lifting Equipment Inspector to 
the site. Scope of proof load testing shall include inhibition of overload protection system (where fitted) to 
allow lifting of overload and verification of its proper adjustment and functionality after removal of inhibition. 
 
 NON-DESTRUCTIVE TESTING (NDT) TECHNIQUES 
On completion of the tests, a thorough examination of the forklift will be undertaken by the competent 
person to ensure no damage or permanent deformation has taken place, the competent person might 
consider it appropriate to apply NDT techniques to assess the integrity of components. These techniques

PILLAR/JIB CRANES 
A Jib/Pillar crane is a type of crane where a horizontal member (Jib Arm), supporting a moveable hoist, 
is fixed to a wall or to a floor-mounted Pillar. The Jib may swing through an arc, to give additional lateral 
movement, or be fixed.

STRUCTURE AND CRANE COMPONENTS 
Inspect the entire crane for structure (such as beam, pillar, post and any other supporting structures) 
damage, check for distortion or cracks in the structural attachment to the upper and lower works of the 
crane. All mounting points whether wall or floor shall be checked to ensure strength is sufficient. Any 
repairs/ Alterations on the crane structure shall be done as per approved OEM procedures or in 
consultation with qualified structural engineer.

operation of all controls (including any remote controls) to determine whether the equipment operates 
correctly and smoothly; and is free from wear and other damage. Functional testing shall be carried out

condition. The drum end of the rope shall be anchored to the drum by an arrangement provided by the 
crane manufacturer.

LIMITERS AND PROTECTIONS 
Check the following for damage, malfunction, etc. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
FUNCTIONAL TEST 
Functional testing shall be carried out without a load applied. The object of functional testing is to 
determine whether the equipment performs as the manufacturer intended. This should include the 
operation of all controls (including any remote controls) to determine whether the equipment operates 
correctly and smoothly; and is free from wear and other damage. Functional testing shall be carried out 
on all functions of the hoist to check all motions alarms, limit switches, safety devices and brakes where 
it might be necessary to include the lifting of a suitable load once these devices have been tested without 
a load applied. For electric operated hoist, it is a requirement that the load can be lowered under control 
with the power off. 
 
 
PERFORMANCE TEST 
Performance testing shall be carried out after functional testing and with the rated load applied. The 
object of performance testing is to determine whether the equipment performs to the manufacturer’s 
specification. This should include the operation of all controls (including any remote controls) to 
determine whether the equipment operates correctly and smoothly at the rated speeds; and is free from 
wear and other damage. Performance testing shall be carried out on all functions of the hoist including 
brakes and safety devices. The hoist shall be capable of sustaining the load with the power off. 
 
 
LOAD TEST 
Before and after any load testing the equipment shall be thoroughly examined. The objective of load 
testing is to determine whether the equipment is stable, structurally sound and fit for the use for which it 
was designed. The test program drawn up by the competent person should include every load-bearing 
part. The competent person should determine whether the original or if appropriate the previous test 
certificate is satisfactory, and subsequent testing should be based on this with any necessary 
modifications to suit the requirements of the particular item of equipment. To prevent undue repetition, 
the test program should be conducted so that each load-bearing part is given one overload. 
During testing with overloads all operations shall be carried out with extreme care and every permissible 
hoist & trolley motion carried out singly at the lowest possible speed. Overloads shall be kept as close to 
the Platform/ground as possible, generally between 100 mm and 200 mm. It is preferred that overloads 
are not raised above 200 mm to allow them to pass over obstructions. To ensure that the load is applied 
at the correct position, any dimensions shall be checked using separate measuring equipment; the crane 
instrumentation shall not be used for this purpose. All measuring equipment should be accurate to ±1 % 
of the measured value unless specified otherwise and be undamaged and properly maintained. The 
weight of the lifting accessories shall be included as part of the test load. All limit switches and overload 
protection systems should be checked and adjusted as necessary.  
Note: The competent person might wish to consult the manufacturer or other design authority to ensure 
that the selected test program is adequate.

GENERAL 
Pneumatic chain Hoist is designed for vertical lifting. Pneumatic chain hoists are normally having 
suspension hook on top which is used in conjunction with beam trolleys and/or Suspension points (A 
‘Lifting Point’ is generic term for the ‘certified’ point(s) or attachment(s) on an item of plant, by which it 
can be safely lifted. The term also applies to points or attachments fixed to structural members and from 
which a load can be suspended.  For example, lifting lugs, lifting eyes, trunnions, fabricated lifting plates, 
pad eyes.

parts affected by the repair/location change are subjected to the test loads in accordance with the original 
hoist specification. 
 
Note: It might be necessary to confirm with the manufacturer or other design authority the loads to be 
applied following such alterations or repairs. 
 
 
PROOF LOAD TEST CRITERIA  
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12 
 
Third Party Inspection Company to advice the required Action Plan for the proof load test and to be sent 
to ADNOC Group for review and approval prior to mobilize the Third Party Lifting Equipment Inspector 
to the site. Scope of proof load testing shall include inhibition of overload protection system (where fitted) 
to allow lifting of overload and verification of its proper adjustment and functionality after removal of 
inhibition. 
 
 NON-DESTRUCTIVE TESTING (NDT) TECHNIQUES 
On completion of the tests, a thorough examination of the hoist will be undertaken by the competent 
person to ensure no damage or permanent deformation has taken place, the competent person shall 
request to apply appropriate NDT techniques to assess the integrity of components. These techniques 
can assist in the detection of any material cracks or defects that might grow in service and ultimately lead 
to failure. Third Party Lifting Equipment Inspector will advise for the required load bearing parts of the 
lifting equipment that need NDT and the recommended NDT technique.

RUNWAY BEAMS 
Runway beams are specially fitted beams, or structural beams that have been tested and certified for 
lifting purposes and the attachment of items of lifting equipment. 
 
Typical runway beam 
 
 PERIODICITY 
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12 
 
 THOROUGH EXAMINATION 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 INSTALLATION AND TRAVELLING MACHINERY (AS APPLICABLE)

## Page 138

Lifting Equipment Thorough Examination 
 
APPENDIX 5 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19

Page 138 of 301 
 PERFORMANCE TEST 
Performance testing shall be carried out with the rated capacity. The object of performance testing is to 
determine whether the equipment performs to the manufacturer’s specification to determine whether the 
equipment operates correctly and smoothly at the rated capacity. The maximum deflection of the simply 
supported runway beam with the rated load at the center of the span, shall not exceed 1/500 of the span, 
for cantilever section of the beam, the maximum deflection with the rated load shall not exceed 1/250 of 
the span. 
 
 LOAD TEST  
Before and after any load testing the equipment shall be thoroughly examined. The objective of load 
testing is to determine whether the equipment is stable, structurally sound and fit for the use for which it 
was designed. The test program drawn up by the competent person should include every load-bearing 
part. The competent person should determine whether the original or if appropriate the previous test 
certificate is satisfactory, and subsequent testing should be based on this with any necessary 
modifications to suit the requirements of the particular item of equipment. To prevent undue repetition,  
the test program should be conducted so that each load-bearing part is given one overload, for example 
to test a wire rope hoist mechanism and brakes it is only necessary to subject the mechanism to the 
overload on maximum line pull. 
Note: The competent person might wish to consult the manufacturer or other design authority to ensure 
that the selected test program is adequate. 
 
During testing with overloads all operations shall be carried out with extreme care and every permissible 
trolley motion carried out singly at the lowest possible speed. Overloads shall be kept as close to the 
Platform/ground as possible, generally between 100 mm and 200 mm. It is preferred that overloads are 
not raised above 200 mm to allow them to pass over obstructions. Where such limitations exist the 
competent person should consider an alternative test to prove the runway beam in the restricted area. 
When no alternative test is possible and the competent person is not satisfied that all of the runway beam 
duties have been adequately tested, use of the runway beam should be restricted to the tested duties 
and both the certificate of test and report of thorough examination endorsed accordingly.  To ensure that 
the load is applied at the correct position, any dimensions shall be checked using separate measuring 
equipment; all measuring equipment should be accurate to ±1 % of the measured value unless specified 
otherwise and be undamaged and properly maintained. The weight of the lifting accessories shall be 
included as part of the test load. During the test the performance of the runway beam should be monitored 
by the competent person. 
 RUNWAY BEAM AND OTHER EQUIPMENT WHICH HAVE BEEN ALTERED OR REPAIRED 
In the event of any alteration or repair that could affect the structural stability of the runway beam, stability 
testing shall be carried out in accordance with the original runway beam specification. In the event of any 
alteration or repair which affects the strength of the runway beam, proof load testing shall be carried out 
to ensure that all parts affected by the repair are subjected to the test loads in accordance with the original 
runway beam specification. 
Note: It might be necessary to confirm with the manufacturer or other design authority the loads to be 
applied following such alterations or repairs. 
 
 PROOF LOAD TEST CRITERIA 
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12 
 
Third Party Inspection Company to advice the required Action Plan for the proof load test and to be sent 
to ADNOC Group for review and approval prior to mobilize the Third Party Lifting Equipment Inspector 
to the site.

 FUNCTIONAL TEST 
Functional testing shall be carried out without a load applied. The object of functional testing is to 
determine whether the equipment performs as the manufacturer intended. This should include the 
operation of all controls to determine whether the equipment operates correctly and smoothly; and is free 
from wear and other damage. Functional testing shall be carried out on all functions of the floor crane to 
check safety devices and brakes where it might be necessary to include the lifting of a suitable load once 
these devices have been tested without a load applied. 
 
 PERFORMANCE TEST 
Performance testing shall be carried out after functional testing and with the rated load applied. The 
object of performance testing is to determine whether the equipment performs to the manufacturer’s 
specification. This should include the operation of all controls to determine whether the equipment 
operates correctly and smoothly at the rated speeds and is free from wear and other damage. 
Performance testing shall be carried out on all functions of the floor crane including brakes and safety 
devices. 
 
 LOAD TEST 
Before and after any load testing the equipment shall be thoroughly examined. The objective of load 
testing is to determine whether the equipment is stable, structurally sound and fit for the use for which it 
was designed. The test program drawn up by the competent person should include every load-bearing 
part. The competent person should determine whether the original or if appropriate the previous test 
certificate is satisfactory, and subsequent testing should be based on this with any necessary 
modifications to suit the requirements of the particular item of equipment.  
Note: The competent person might wish to consult the manufacturer or other design authority to ensure 
that the selected test program is adequate. 
 
 PROOF LOAD TEST CRITERIA:  
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12 
 
 NON-DESTRUCTIVE TESTING (NDT) TECHNIQUES 
On completion of the tests, a thorough examination of the floor crane will be undertaken by the competent 
person to ensure no damage or permanent deformation has taken place, the competent person might 
consider it appropriate to apply NDT techniques to assess the integrity of components. These techniques 
can assist in the detection of any material cracks or defects that might grow in service and ultimately lead 
to failure. Third Party Lifting Equipment Inspector will advise for the required load bearing parts of the 
lifting equipment that need NDT and the recommended NDT technique.

SNATCH BLOCKS OR MULTIPLE SHEAVE BLOCKS 
 GENERAL 
Snatch blocks or multiple sheave blocks are available in the form of single sheave or multi-sheave with 
a choice of head fittings, the most popular being swivel shackle or swivel oval eye type head fittings. 
The main function of a single sheave block is to change the direction of the hoisting or pulling rope Multi- 
sheave blocks, when rigged in the normally used ‘advantage’ mode, reduce the pull required on the lead 
rope to lift a load.  The pull required on the lead rope reduces as the number of falls (sheaves) increases. 
 
 PERIODICITY 
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12 
 
 THOROUGH EXAMINATION

HAND POWERED LIFTING EQUIPMENT - CHAIN BLOCKS, LEVER HOISTS AND JAW 
WINCHES (WIRE ROPE PULLING MACHINE)  
 GENERAL 
 
Manual chain hoist or chain block is a lifting machine, which, through the use of a gearbox and 
brake mechanism, allows the user to raise heavy loads vertically by applying a force to the 
operating chain (hand chain). 
 
In the case of an angular lift or horizontal pulling operation having to be undertaken the Lifting 
competent person should in the first instance consider other more suitably designed types of Lifting 
Equipment such as Lever Hoist and Tirfor (Wire Rope Pulling Machine) for angular lifting and 
horizontal pulling operations. 
Where Chain Blocks are the only feasible option for use for an angular Lifting operation, such as 
when a long fall or high height of lift is required to be undertaken then it is recommended that the 
following safe operation guidelines are adhered to: 
 
The SWL of a Chain Block is de-rated to 50% of its SWL for all non-vertical angular lifts.  
 
The maximum angle of operation for any Chain Block safe operation is limited to a maximum 
of 45° from the vertical. 
The above conditions are not applicable if the Lifting Plan is engineered with Lift Analysis prepared by 
the Contractor and approved by Structural Engineer. 
 
It is also important to highlight that light loads less than 10% of SWL as applied to a Chain Block SHALL 
NOT be lifted e.g. a 1.0 t SWL Chain Block should not lift anything less than 100 kg , 2.0 t; 200 kg , 5.0 
t;  500 kg  and so forth. This is due to weight of the light load possibly not being enough to activate the 
friction brake. 
 
 
Manually operated lever hoist is a lifting machine, which, through the use of a gearbox and brake 
mechanism, allows the user to raise heavy loads   by applying a force to the operating lever.

## Page 146

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations 
Lifting Equipment Thorough Examination 
 
APPENDIX 5 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 146 of 301 
Jaw winches are usually of the friction grip on cable type. This type of machine develops a pulling 
force on the load rope by a combination of alternate gripping and pulling (one grips while the other 
pulls) by two sets of jaws. The jaw mechanisms are operated by an external hand lever. Most other 
lifting and pulling machines that use a wire rope have a similar principle of operation. 
 
 PERIODICITY 
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12 
 
 THOROUGH EXAMINATION 
 
 
Ensure that the chain block is marked with a unique Identification Number or Asset Number, 
SWL, COLOR code and have valid certificate of inspection from an ADNOC approved Third 
Party using approved Lifting Equipment Inspector. Check load chain for bent, stretched or 
corroded links;  
 
Check hand and load chain pocket wheels are in good order and free from debris;  
 
Check body assembly for damage;  
 
Suspension and load hook safety latches shall function correctly, and swivels operate 
without restriction.  Ensure that there is no obvious opening-out of the hook(s);  
 
Check load chain for distortion, corrosion and any other form of damage. Never attempt to 
straighten a distorted chain link.  A distorted link may have been already damaged beyond 
repair and will be unfit for use;  
 
If the hoist has not been used for some time, the load chain may be dry and shall be spray 
coated with WD40 or light machine oil. Take care not to contaminate brake mechanism. The 
chain shall not however, be over-lubricated as contamination of the hoist brake discs could 
result;  
 
If visible, check ratchet pawl and ratchet wheel for correct operation, damage and distortion.  
Similarly check for damage and distortion to any other visible mechanisms;  
 
Check the load chain for any obvious signs of damage, wear, excessive corrosion, cracks or 
distortion, signs of elongation (Refer to Section 4.3.9 of this Appendix); and 
 
Check for localized wear on chains, and the wear shall not exceed 8% of the original 
diameter of the chain and its fittings. 
 
 
Ensure that the Lever hoist is marked with a unique Identification Number or Asset Number, 
SWL, COLOR code and have valid certificate of inspection from an ADNOC approved Third 
Party using approved Lifting Equipment Inspector;  
 
Where fitted, check that the pawl that operates correctly, in raising, lowering (engaged) and 
'pull through' (disengaged) modes of operation. When engaged the pawl gives a distinctive 
'clicking' sound when the ratchet lever block is operated;  
 
Check chain, load wheel and chain end stop. To prevent the end of the chain from passing 
through manually operated lever hoist, an end stop shall be present on the free end of the 
load chain;  
 
Hang the manually operated lever hoist on its suspension hook and pull the load chain out 
to its 'stop' and let it hang vertically free. The chain linkage shall all appear to be in a straight 
line; if the chain gives the appearance of having a 'spiral' the manually operated lever hoist 
has been overloaded whilst the chain was twisted.  There shall be no damaged, corroded or 
distorted components;  
 
Check for build-up of dirt in the pocket wheel;

## Page 147

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations 
Lifting Equipment Thorough Examination 
 
APPENDIX 5 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 147 of 301 
 
Check correct operation of selection lever;  
 
Check the suspension hook and load hook. Ensure hook safety latch functions correctly and 
the swivel operates without restriction. Check also for obvious opening-out, of either the load 
hook or the suspension hook;  
 
Check load chain for distortion, corrosion and any other form of damage;  
 
If the hoist has not been used for some time the load chain could be dry, spray with light 
machine oil;  
 
Check the load chain for any obvious signs of damage, wear, excessive corrosion, cracks or 
distortion, signs of elongation (Refer to Section 4.3.9 of this Appendix); and 
 
Check for localized wear on chains, and the wear shall not exceed 8% of the original 
diameter of the chain and its fittings. 
 
 
Ensure that the Jaw winch is marked with a unique Identification Number or Asset Number, 
SWL, COLOR code and have valid certificate of inspection from an ADNOC approved Third 
Party using approved Lifting Equipment Inspector;  
 
Generally check the machine and inspect for corrosion, deterioration and any other form of 
damage;  
 
Jaw winches shall only be operated with the correct size wire rope. A Tirfor will not operate 
safely or efficiently if any other form of wire rope is used;    
 
Check that the shear pins are in position in the operating lever/spindle and that spare shear 
pins are present in the hollow shaft of the operating lever stub. Only shear pins supplied by 
the manufacturer shall be used in the operating lever of the Jaw winches The shear pin is 
intended to fail and protect the Jaw winches mechanisms from overload in the event of 
excessive force being applied to the operating lever;  
 
Check condition of Jaw winches anchor point;  
 
Check for wear in the jaws as follows: 
1. 
With no rope fitted (and lever in 'released' position) mark on the machine case the 
position of the rope release lever. 
2. 
Insert the correct rope into the machine and move the rope release lever to the 'jaws 
closed' position and make a second mark at the position of the rope release lever. 
3. 
The distance between the two marks shall not be less than the manufactures stated 
limits. 
4. 
If the distance between the two marks is less than that stated by the manufacturer, 
wear on the rope jaws is indicated and the machine shall not be used. 
 
Thoroughly check the wire rope as follows: 
1. 
Ensure that the rope has no kinks. A kinked load rope will cause the machine to 
malfunction. 
2. 
Wipe the rope clean before fitting it to the machine and at the same time examine the 
rope for wear and damage.  A damaged rope shall not be used. 
3. 
Check for localized wear on wire, and the wear shall not exceed 10% of the original 
diameter. 
 
Ensure that the machine is well lubricated. To lubricate both jaws of the machine place the 
reversing lever in a central position and using oil can 'squirt' medium gear oil into the 
mechanism, through the slot in the top of the casing, in front of and behind the reversing 
lever.

THOROUGH EXAMINATION AND TESTING OF LIFTED EQUIPMENT 
Every lifting appliance and every piece of lifting accessory shall be thoroughly examined and where 
necessary, tested by a competent person before it is used for first time, after any substantial repair or 
modification and thereafter at regular intervals. 
 
 
CARGO CARRYING UNIT (CCU) CONTAINER, CARGO BASKET, GAS BOTTLE RACK, 
TOOLBOXES, WASTE SKIPS, SKIDS ETC. 
 
GENERAL 
All CCUs and lifting gear used to ship materials to or from offshore installations are correctly chosen for 
the purpose, in terms of type, size and load carrying capacity. They should satisfy themselves that a 
testing and inspection procedure is in place. All certification shall be in date at the time of use; and has 
sufficient test period remaining so as to prevent the CCU certification expiring when offshore – normally 
one month minimum. 
Containers with corner castings, which are employed as a means of lifting the container, should not be 
presented for shipment. Containers that are lifted using corner castings are, in reality, ISO containers, 
and are designed for use in general marine transport, loading and unloading in ports and inland 
waterways, by means of a four-point vertical lifting device i.e. spreader beam. All CCUs should be 
provided with complete lifting sets in place. CCU doors shall be properly secured, with both top and 
bottom locking cams fully engaged, and door handle locking mechanism secured with a seal. With open 
CCUs, care should be taken to ensure the drainage holes are clean and free of debris. CCU units shall 
be clean and free of debris prior to loading. 
Cargo restraining nets should be used in all closed CCU’s. Ensure that nets are the correct size and type 
for the CCU and that the fixing points and nets are in good condition. 
Check CCU roof, forklift pockets and any external ledges for loose items such as tools, stones, etc. 
Always check lifting sets and fixed lifting points. 
Check the container door(s) are closed, dogs (cams/claws) top and bottom can be fully engaged and the 
closing mechanism secured so that it cannot inadvertently come open during handling and transport. 
Always load heavier cargo low in the container, with lighter goods on top. For ease of removal, heavier 
items should be loaded at the front of closed containers. Particularly heavy items should be shipped in 
open top units, i.e. half heights. Ensure CCU contents are lashed or wedged securely, to avoid movement 
in transit. Use cargo restraining nets in all closed CCUs. Ensure that nets are the correct size and type 
for the CCU and that the fixing points and nets are in good condition. Ensure that no equipment is loaded 
above the height of an open CCU without a risk assessment. This is to prevent snagging, damage to 
contents and potential dropped objects. The use of a net, tarpaulin, wooden battens or roof bars is 
recommended to mitigate the risk of snagging. 
 
 
PERIODICITY  
Refer to Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12 
 
 
THOROUGH EXAMINATION 
All CCU’s including Cargo baskets, Gas bottle racks, Tool boxes, Waste skips, skids, Air Compressor 
Frames, Tank Frames …etc. shall have 4 Lifting Pad-eyes and be fabricated in accordance with either 
BS EN ISO 10855:2018/DNVGL-ST-E271/ABS Guide for Offshore Containers (for Maximum Gross 
Weight of up to 25 t) or DNVGL-ST-E273 (for Maximum Gross Weight more than 25 t). 
 
Where the lifting set, intended for use with the container, is used for the lifting test, care shall be taken to 
ensure that no overloading, deformation or distortion is induced in the lifting set. If the lifting set normally 
fitted to the container be used for the lifting test it shall be visually inspected after the load test by an 
inspection body as per the requirements of this document. 
The container shall be carefully lifted in such a way that no significant acceleration forces occur. It shall 
be held for 5 minutes before measurements are taken.

The visual inspection shall be of the exterior and the interior of the container without cargo to ensure that 
the container is fit for its intended use. All load bearing parts, especially the base structure, shall be 
inspected. For containers with fixed equipment, the inspection body shall determine whether access to 
load bearing parts is adequate. 
 
The inspection shall be carried out in a situation providing sufficient lighting and other facilities necessary 
to allow it to be carried out safely and effectively. The facility shall include suitable means of lifting and 
supporting the container for the purposes of inspecting the under-side. 
 
 
 
 
All pad eyes and lashing points shall be visually inspected for distortion, mechanical damage or 
any other sign of distress or overload. 
In order to prevent lateral bending moments on pad eyes, they shall be aligned with the sling to 
the center of lift, with a maximum manufacturing tolerance of ± 2.5. Any difference in the diagonal 
measurements between lifting point centers shall not exceed 0.2 % of the length of the diagonal, 
or 5 mm, whichever is the greater. The diameter of holes in pad eyes shall match the shackle used, 
clearance between shackle pin and pad eye hole shall not exceed 6 % of the nominal shackle pin 
diameter. However, maximum concentrated stresses at the hole edges shall not exceed 2 x Re at 
design load. 
Note-1: It is recommended that the clearance actually provided be as close as practicable to the 
6% limit. The tolerance between pad eye thickness and inside width of shackle shall not exceed 
25 % of the inside width of the shackle. Pad eyes shall be so designed as to permit free movement 
of the shackle and sling termination without fouling the pad eye. 
Pad eyes shall not protrude outside the boundaries of the container other than vertically upward 
and shall as far as possible be designed to avoid damage from other containers. Lifting points shall 
be positioned on the container to preclude, as far as practicable, the risk of slings fouling against 
the container or its cargo during normal use. 
Pad eyes shall be welded to the frame with full penetration welds. If the lifting force is transferred 
through the thickness of a plate, plates with specified through thickness properties in accordance 
with EN 10164 shall be used. 
Note-2: It is recommended that pad-eyes be slotted into the primary structure. 
Note-3: Where ISO-corner fittings are mounted in conjunction with pad eyes, the corner fittings 
are not intended for lifting with slings.  
Note-4: Containers with ISO lifting points shall not be lifted by using shackles; however, it is 
accepted temporarily if a dedicated spreader bar with Vertical Slings and anti-twist block (Cam 
lock) are supplied with the equipment. 
Note-5: Attention is drawn to the need for designers to be aware of the restrictions in permissible 
shackle types introduced in BS EN ISO 10855-2:2018 and particularly to the preference for bow 
shackles with bolt type pin with hexagon head, hexagon nut and split cotter pin. As a result, it is 
necessary that the designer ensure sufficient clearance surrounding the pad eye to enable the 
fitting and removal of this preferred type. 
 
An ISO (International Organization for Standardization) Container (or Shipping Container, CSC 
Container or Standard Freight Container) is a re-usable transport container, used for international 
traffic and designed to facilitate the carriage of goods by one or more modes of transport (including

marine - but not for handling in open seas) without intermediate reloading. Such containers shall 
be certified and marked according to the International Convention for Safe Containers, CSC. 
Freight containers typically are designed in accordance with ISO standards, and are often known 
as CSC/ISO Containers. 
Guidance Note: 
ISO Freight containers are not suitable for offshore use, due to their overall design limitations of 
‘dynamic’ loading/ stress factors that are ‘at play’ when unloading/back loading in an open seas 
environment and also due to the incompatibility of the top four ISO Corner Castings for the purpose 
of safely and securely attaching a shackle and the associated sling lifting set (as is required to lift 
on/off an offshore installation). Shackle attachment onto an ISO Corner Casting does not provide 
safe or adequate load distribution area transfer to the shackle pin. Shackle pins and ISO Corner 
Castings are subjected to ‘point loading’ stresses because of this fact. 
 
The structure shall be visually inspected for corrosion, mechanical damage or deformation. 
 
Doors, frames, seals, hinges, locks, etc. shall be visually inspected and functionally checked to 
ensure that they operate in a satisfactory manner without undue force being required. 
 
The floor shall be visually inspected to check that it is not deformed and that it shows no signs of 
distress or overload. Drainage facilities, where fitted, shall be inspected, e.g. drain holes shall be 
clear of debris. 
 
 
Check the inspection & test certificates are valid and it shall be certified by an ADNOC 
approved Third Party Company using approved Lifting Equipment Inspector;  
 
Pre-trip inspection — required checks Fail or Pass;  
 
Check data plate to ensure that the inspection date is valid;   
 
Check safety markings to ensure they meet the standard requirements;  
 
 
 
Check lifting set for obvious signs of damage;  
 
 
 
Check lifting set to establish that all parts are present, correct, properly connected & secured;  
 
Check container roof, forklift pockets, frames, etc. for loose items;  
 
 
Check that safety nets are in position where necessary and that tie down points are in place 
and are fit for purpose;  
 
 
 
Check container doors are closed, secured and locking mechanism undamaged; and 
 
Check drainage holes are clear on open containers. 
 
PROOF LOAD TEST   
Refer to Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12 and Details as mentioned below. 
 
 
 
Type Testing: 
Type Tests shall be carried out on the newly fabricated equipment to verify the design. 
However, Sample Equipment (Refer to Table below) will only be selected for the Three Type 
Tests as below. All Equipment shall be proof load tested with Load = 2.5 x Maximum Gross 
Weight. NDT shall be carried out pre and post the proof load test.

General:  
A container selected for type testing shall be representative of the production units and not 
a hand-built pre-production development container. It shall be built in conformity with plans 
and data and using tooling comparable to those planned for subsequent production. The 
test masses/test load shall normally be evenly distributed inside the container. If it is not 
possible to place the entire test mass inside the container, some of it may be placed outside 
or under the container, provided that this gives a loading on the structure similar to the 
distribution of the container loading in operating condition. 
 
Lifting tests:  
 
All-point lifting: The container shall be loaded to give a total mass of 2.5 x Maximum 
Gross Weight and to be lifted clear of the ground, using all the pad eyes. 
Note: This total mass may be obtained by putting in an internal test load of 2.5 x 
Maximum Gross Weight minus Tare Weight. No deflections during testing shall be 
greater than 1/300 of the span of the member. The offshore container shall show no 
permanent deformation or other damage after testing. 
 
Two-point lifting: An offshore container fitted with four pad eyes shall also be lifted 
from only two pad eyes, situated diagonally opposite each other, with a total mass of 
1.5 x Maximum Gross Weight. The offshore container shall show no permanent 
deformation or other damage after testing.  
Note-Post-lifting test inspection and examination: On completion of the lifting test, 
an NDT and visual inspection of the pad-eyes shall be carried out. 
 
Vertical impact test: The container, with its internal test mass corresponding to 
payload (SWL), shall be either lowered or dropped on to a workshop floor of concrete 
or other rigid structure. 
Note-1: This floor may be covered with a sheathing of wooden planks with a thickness 
not exceeding 50 mm. 
Note-2: If the container is lowered from a crane, the suspending wire and hook may 
dampen the impact compared to a free-fall drop test. Therefore, the impact speed 
should be greater if a lowering test is used. 
In both cases, the container shall be so inclined that each of the bottom side and end 
rails connected to the lowest corner forms an angle of not less than 5° with the floor. 
However, the greatest height difference between the highest and lowest point of the 
underside of the container corners need not be more than 400 mm. 
Note-3: The impacting corner shall be the one expected to have the lowest rigidity. 
On closed dry cargo containers this will normally be at the door end. No significant 
permanent damage shall occur. 
Note-4: Cracks in welds and minor deformations may be repaired. 
One of the following procedures shall be carried out: 
 
Drop test: An internal load equal to the payload (SWL) shall be safely secured and 
the container shall be inclined as described above. The container shall be suspended 
from a quick release hook. When released, the container shall drop freely for at least 
50 mm to give it a speed at initial impact of at least 1 m/s. 
 
Lowering test: An internal load equal to the payload (SWL) shall be safely secured 
and the container shall be inclined as described above. The container shall be lowered 
to the floor at a constant speed of not less than 1.5 m/s. 
 
Other tests: 
 
Open top containers with an overall length of 6.5 m or more, with fork pockets 
designed for loaded lifting shall be loaded to a total uniformly distributed gross 
mass of 1.6 x (Maximum Gross Weight + Lifting Set Weight) and lifted clear of

be made of corrosion resistant material securely attached externally in a manner designed to avoid 
unauthorized or accidental removal. The plate shall be fitted to a door, or, on containers with no doors, 
in a prominent position.

SELECTION OF CCU’S LIFTING SET 
 
Items of integrated lifting equipment used to connect the offshore container to the lifting appliance. 
This can comprise one or multi leg slings (with or without a top leg) and shackles. 
General Requirements and Precautions: 
Checks prior to the attachment and use of slings and shackles include: 
 
Confirm that color coding is current and the sling has a visible unique number and Safe 
Working Load (SWL) mark. 
 
Examine for wear, corrosion, abrasion and mechanical damage, which may render the 
sling(s) unsafe. 
 
Check that the sling set is correctly fitted, e.g. no twists in the legs. 
 
Confirm that the SWL capacity for the sling set at each side of load is adequate for the entire 
weight to be lifted. 
 
Check that pin size and type is correct for the type of shackle. 
 
Check that the appropriate securing arrangements are installed (split pins, wire housing, 
etc.). 
 
The preferred style of shackle is the bow or anchor type fitted with a safety pin that is, bolt, 
nut and split pin. The bolt or pin shall be of the same material as the body of the shackle.

Ensure the correct type of shackle bolt or pin is fitted. A common problem exists where 
missing high-grade shackle pins are replaced with standard bolts that are not of the same 
material as the body and are not capable of taking the designated load. 
The crane hook link (Master Link) shall have a minimum dimension of 270 mm x 140 mm to 
minimize the risk of trapped fingers when attaching the hook to the lifting arrangements. 
It is preferable to use Wire Rope Slings rather than Chain Slings with CCU. 
 
General precautions when using lifting arrangements made up using wire slings include: 
 
All sling legs, which are connected directly to master links or to shackles, shall have thimbles 
fitted to reduce the bend radius on the wire and increase their serviceable life. 
 
To avoid out-of-plane forces, the maximum number of lifting legs allowed on a single master 
link or ring is two. It is permissible to have a third leg on this ring, but only if it is to be used 
as a top lifting leg. Three and four leg lifting slings shall be fitted to quadruple assemblies, ie 
a main lifting link with two sub-links. 
 
CCUs shall be fitted with either a four or five leg lifting assembly with master link, depending 
on CCU type. The fifth leg is designed to hang over the side of the unit to assist in its hook-
up, eliminating the need for personnel having to climb on top of containers to attach or 
release the crane hook. 
 
This fifth leg is not a design requirement, but it is a preferred option and, if fitted, should “fall 
short” of the height of the container to facilitate connection and release of the hook. If the 
dimensions of the container are such that the fifth leg would present a snagging hazard at 
other stages of the supply chain, the fifth leg should not be fitted. 
 
Operationally, it may be necessary to use wire rope of a smaller diameter however, wire rope 
of a smaller diameter is susceptible to impact damage and appropriate checks should be 
carried out. 
 
To allow the lifting set to withstand the maximum dynamic loadings applied during offshore 
operations an enhancement factor is given in each offshore container standard. This 
enhancement factor is applied to the gross weight of the offshore container to determine the 
minimum working load (WLL min) or safe working load required for the associated lifting set. 
In the previous manufacturing standard, i.e. BS 7072, the enhancement factor was 
recognized as 1.3 x gross weight. Although this enhancement factor is easy to apply, it does 
not truly reflect the relationship between the dynamic load and the containers’ gross weight. 
This is resolved in BS EN ISO 10855:2018, DNVGL-ST-E271:2017 and ABS Guide for 
Offshore Containers by the introduction of variable enhancement factors. The working load 
limit of the lifting sets for offshore containers shall be determined using the table in the next 
page (i.e. Table to calculate the minimum WLL by multiplying the container rating with the 
Enhancement Factor). Except for containers with ratings below 2000 kg, the container rating 
R shall be multiplied by an enhancement factor to give the WLL min of the lifting set. For 
intermediate container ratings the working load limit values shall be interpolated. 
The minimum working load limit of each shackle (WLLs) as per BS EN ISO 10855:2018 shall 
be calculated as given in (Appendix 5 - Table 7.1.3) and the slings shall be selected as per 
BS EN ISO 10855:2018 Enhancement Factor Table mentioned in (Appendix 5 - Table 7.1.4) 
below.

The lifting set shall be of sufficient length to allow easy handling by operators. The top link or

Shackles shall be Grade 6 or Grade 8 and meet all requirements of EN 13889 or EN 1677-1 or 
ABNT NBR 13545 with the additional requirement that the tolerance on the nominal diameter of 
the shackle pin shall be 0 to +3 %. 
Shackles shall be restricted to: Bolt type pin with hexagon head, hexagon nut and split cotter pin.

Slings shall be marked with an identification tag permanently attached to the top assembly of the 
sling. The tag shall be made of metal with the marking permanently embossed or stamped. The 
tag shall be 8-sided for chain slings and round for wire rope slings. 
Where two 2-leg slings are selected to function as a 4 leg sling, both shall be marked as a 4 leg 
sling. 
The marking on tags for chain and wire rope slings shall include: 
 
Number of this Standard; 
 
Unique identification number of the sling; 
 
Number of legs; 
 
Diameter of chain or wire rope used, including the top leg where fitted; 
 
Working load limit (WLL) in tonnes; 
 
Maximum angle of the sling legs from the vertical; and  
 
Mass of the lifting set (S) in Kg;

THOROUGH EXAMINATION OF CCU’S LIFTING SET 
The inspection shall be carried out with normally corrected vision, in a situation providing sufficient 
lighting and other facilities necessary to allow it to be carried out safely and effectively. 
 
 
Inspection of chain slings, wire rope slings and components shall be carried out before every use. 
(Refer to Section 8.2 & Section 8.12 of this Appendix) 
 
 
Shackles shall be visually inspected (Refer to Section 8.9 of this Appendix). 
 
 
During service, wire rope slings are subjected to conditions that affect their safety. It is necessary, 
therefore, to ensure, as far as is reasonably practicable, that the sling is safe for continued use.

The sling shall be inspected for any obvious signs of deterioration before each use, If, at any time 
there is reason to doubt the safe condition of the sling, it shall be withdrawn from service and 
subjected to a thorough examination If the tag or label identifying the sling and its working load 
limit becomes detached and the necessary information is not marked on the master link, or by 
some other means, the sling shall be withdrawn from service. 
 
 
An inspection is a visual check on the condition of the sling to identify any obvious damage or 
deterioration that might affect its fitness for use. 
The sling should be withdrawn from service and referred to a competent person for thorough 
examination if any of the following is observed before each use: 
(i) Illegible sling markings i.e. sling identification and/or working load limit;  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
(i) Wire Rope Slings (Refer to Section 8.12 of this Appendix) 
(ii) Shackles (Refer to Section 8.9  of this Appendix) 
 
 
USE OF TAG LINES 
Tag lines shall only be used after a risk assessment has been undertaken to ensure all involved personnel 
standing in safe positions. 
In certain circumstances light, soft lines may be used to assist in the handling of long and/or fragile items 
of cargo. These are often referred to as tag lines. It shall be recognized that, whilst such aids may assist 
operations, their use does introduce some additional risks, as described below. 
 
 
Potential injuries from dropped objects as a result of personnel handling cargo having to 
work in closer proximity to suspended loads than would normally be the case;  
 
Potential injuries result from personnel handling cargo being dragged across the handling 
area, through a heavy load rotating in an uncontrolled manner and the tag line being fouled 
in limbs or clothing; and  
 
Potential injuries resulting from tag lines being secured to adjacent fixed structures parting 
and whipping back as a result of a heavy load rotating in an uncontrolled manner. 
 
 
Tag lines shall be made up from single, continuous lengths of rope;  
 
Apart from the knot attaching the line to the cargo, there shall be no other joints or knots in 
the line; and 
 
Tag lines shall be of sufficient length to allow personnel handling cargo to work in a safe 
position well clear of the immediate vicinity of the load. In this regard it is recommended that

## Page 162

HSE Management System

Effective date: August 2019 
Page 162 of 301 
the length of the line should be not less than one and a half (1.5) times the maximum height 
above the handling area at which the arrangements will be used. 
 
 
Tag lines are an aid to positioning the load when landing, and as such shall only be used 
when weather conditions would permit the lifting of the item without the use of such 
arrangements. It shall not be assumed that, in conditions more severe than this, the use of 
tag lines will allow the operation to be completed safely;  
 
At all times personnel handling tag lines shall work at a horizontal distance from the load 
equivalent to its height above the handling area, maintaining an angle between the line and 
the horizontal of not more than 45°;  
 
All sections of the line, including slack, shall be kept in front of the body, between the handler 
and the load;  
 
Where two or more persons are handling the same line, ALL shall work on the same side of 
the line. Any slack shall be kept in front of the group;  
 
Tag lines shall be held in such a manner that they can be quickly and totally released. They 
shall not be looped around wrists, or other parts of the body; 
 
Particular care shall be taken when using tag lines whilst wearing gloves to ensure that the 
line does not foul the glove; 
 
Tag lines shall not be secured or attached in any manner to adjacent structures or equipment. 
This includes the practice of making a “round turn” on stanchions or similar structures and 
surging the line to control the load; and 
 
Where pre-installed lines are used, consideration should be given to providing personnel 
with boathooks or similar equipment to retrieve the lines without having to approach the 
dangerous area in the vicinity of the suspended load. An example of such circumstances 
would be when lines are attached to a load on the deck of a vessel, the load being then 
transferred to an offshore installation. 
 
TANK CONTAINERS FOR DANGEROUS GOODS 
The tanks used for transportation of dangerous goods shall fulfil the requirements of IMDG code and BS 
EN ISO 10855:2018/DNVGL-ST-E271/ABS Guide for Offshore Containers; and shall be design 
according to a reorganized code for pressure vessel. A tank and its support shall be able to withstand 
lifting and impact loads. In addition, due account shall be taken of fluid surge arising from partly filled 
tank.  
On tank containers for dangerous cargoes, all parts of the tank and fittings shall be suitably protected 
from impact damage. In addition to the requirements as stated above, the following requirements apply:

Note 1: Tanks for dangerous cargoes shall be tested according to the requirements of IMDG code. 
Note 2: Relevant information shall be marked on the tanks in accordance with the IMDG code. 
The dangerous goods transport document shall include a certification or declaration that the consignment 
is acceptable for transport and that the goods are properly packaged, marked and labelled, and in proper 
condition for transport in accordance with the applicable regulations. The text for this certification is:  
"I hereby declare that the contents of this consignment are fully and accurately described above by the  
Proper Shipping Name, and are classified, packaged, marked and labelled/placarded, and are in all 
respects in proper condition for transport according to applicable international and national government 
regulations"   
The certification shall be duly signed, stamped and dated by ADNOC approved Third Party Company 
using approved Lifting Equipment Inspector. 
 
 
OEM EQUIPMENT 
 
 
 
 
 
Equipment with Single Lifting Point shall only be lifted directly in case on-board or onshore lifts and to 
be containerized or modified with four pad eyes on top, enclosed in a frame if cannot be containerized. 
Third Party lifting Equipment Inspector shall clearly mention in his certificate “The Equipment with 
single lifting point shall only lifted on-board and onshore and be containerized if lifting offshore”

based on the successful test report shall be issued by the manufacturer, however an independent Third 
Party Company shall witness all the Type Tests carried out at the manufacturer’s facility for each batch.

PALLET FORK 
 GENERAL 
Pallet Forks are used to lift palletized, or similar, loads suspended from a crane hook. They are usually 
fabricated from hollow section box, plate and flat steel sections. A typical self-balancing Pallet Fork 
attachment is shown in below. 
 
The top eye of the fork shown in above is spring loaded so that the end of the forks maintains a slight 
upward stance when suspended under load but returns to the unloaded position when the load is 
removed. This is to allow easy engagement with the load and then to prevent the load slipping off during 
the lifting and moving operation. Other designs require the operator to position the suspension fitting, 
usually a link or shackle, in the correct position to balance the load at the correct attitude. The fork arms 
usually have adjustable centers for handling varying loads. 
ISO 17096 and BS EN 13155 specify requirements for the Pallet Fork. 
 SAFETY REQUIREMENT 
 
Pallet forks shall be designed to withstand a static load of 2 times the Working Load Limit “WLL” 
without any deformation taking place and with a static load of 3 times the “WLL” without release of 
the load even if permanent deformation occurs. 
 
 
To enable the operator to position the Pallet Fork without the risk of finger injuries, positioning 
handles shall be fitted in an appropriate position unless the design has features that will provide 
natural handholds.

## Page 167

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations 
Lifting Equipment Thorough Examination 
 
APPENDIX 5 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 167 of 301 
 
The design shall incorporate features to prevent the load sliding and becoming displaced or falling. 
Various ways of achieving this are given in the standard, however the choice will largely depend 
on the nature of the load to be lifted and the intended lifting operation. 
 
 
 MANUFACTURING TESTS 
ISO 17096 and BS EN 13155 specifies the requirements of various type tests by manufacturer to prove 
the design on one or more samples and on completion of manufacture, verification of each Pallet Fork.  
 THOROUGH EXAMINATION 
Lifting Pallet Forks fall under the heading of 'lifted equipment' as mentioned in Table 3.1.1 (Classification 
of Lifting Equipment) of this document and therefore shall be examined by a competent person (ADNOC 
approved Lifting Equipment Inspector working in approved TPA in UAE) at periods not exceeding 6 
months and Color Coding as per ADNOC Standard. 
The requirements for load tests to be made as part of the examination will vary dependent on the 
circumstances. If a structural repair has been made a load test shall be carried out, but great care will be 
needed to ensure that any load is applied correctly so as not to damage it. 
NDT (MPI) shall be carried out after repair and Proof Load Test; however, ADNOC approved Third Party 
Lifting Equipment Inspector may request NDT (MPI) at any time during the 6-months thorough 
examination. 
Where load testing is deemed necessary, before any load is applied the manufacturer's instructions shall 
be obtained and then strictly followed. Under no circumstances should the tester and examiner apply an 
overload without taking these steps to establish the correct load as damage or distortion may result. 
The general appearance of the Pallet Forks shall be considered during the examination, in particular 
paying attention to the following: 
(a) 
Bending and distortion, mechanical/physical damage and corrosion of the structural members. In 
particular attention shall be paid to hollow sections which may have dents or localized buckling, to 
the flanges of structural rolled steel sections and to plate sections which may be bent; 
(b) 
Fixings and welds. Welds and adjacent areas shall be examined for cracks, bolts and rivets 
examined for wear, distortion, corrosion, damage and tightness; 
(c) 
Lifting eyes, load attachment points etc. looking for wear, distortion, elongation of eyes and other 
physical damage; and 
(d) 
Spring assisted tilt mechanism, the condition and correct working of the spring. 
 MARKING 
ISO 17096 and BS EN 13155 require Pallet Forks to be permanently and legibly marked with the following 
minimum information:

GENERAL

Horizontal Plate Clamps: These clamps are used in pairs and usually suspended by a two-leg sling

EYE BOLTS 
 
GENERAL 
There are three types of eye bolts available in the field i.e. Collar type eye bolt, Eye bolt with link and 
Dynamo type Eye bolt.

## Page 171

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations 
Lifting Equipment Thorough Examination 
 
APPENDIX 5 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 171 of 301 
 
Eyebolts with link can, in all respects, be considered as the general-purpose type, used especially 
whenever the loading cannot be confined to the plane of the eye. They may be loaded in any 
direction up to the marked safe working load, provided that the angle of the load to the axis of the 
screw thread of the eyebolt does not exceed 15°. Eyebolts with link may be used for non-axial 
loading at inclinations greater than 15° provided the SWL is appropriately reduced (Refer to BS EN 
ISO 3266). 
The eyebolt with link is designed to accept a sling hook without the need for an intermediate 
component. 
 
(c) 
Dynamo eyebolt: The dynamo eyebolt is intended for axial lifting only. Loading by even 5° out of 
the axial, cause undue stress in the screw thread and shank. Where axial Lifting cannot be ensured, 
a collar eyebolt or an eyebolt with link shall be used. 
 
 
FITTING PAIRS OF COLLAR EYE BOLTS 
The plane of the eye of each of a pair of collar eyebolts shall ideally be within ±5° of the plane containing 
the axes of the two eyebolts. If at first this condition is not fulfilled, it can be achieved by the insertion of 
shims which should not exceed in thickness, half the pitch of the relevant screw thread, or by machining 
the contacting surface but not the underside of the eyebolt collar. Under no circumstances shall the 
eyebolt be over-tightened in an attempt to achieve correct alignment. Care shall also be taken to avoid 
misalignment whereby the application of the angular load tends to unscrew the eyebolt from its seating. 
 
 
PERIODICITY  
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12. 
 
 
THOROUGH EXAMINATION 
 
 
 
 
 
 
PROOF LOAD TEST  
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12.

GENERAL 
Generally man-made fiber slings are used for slinging fragile loads or for suspending loads where the 
equipment coating has to be protected i.e. chrome tubular.

Generally, the width of a webbing sling governs the WLL and approximates to 1 tonne per 50 mm of 
width for Simplex (single thickness) slings and 1 tonne WLL per 25 mm width for Duplex (double 
thickness) slings. 
Man-made fiber (webbing) slings are manufactured in various formats e.g. as flat web slings with soft 
eyes, eyes with links or endless to suit specific requirements. They can also be supplied as “round” slings. 
Webbing slings are manufactured from Polyester, Nylon or Polypropylene. Each material has particular 
design properties, each of which shall be considered and may affect a webbing sling selection process.

Note-1: It is not recommended to use Synthetic Slings for Materials Lifting to/from the Supply Boat; 
however, it can be used with metal ring within the Platform or the Rig subject to Risk Assessment. 
Note-2: It is also not recommended to use Round Slings for shipment offshore, due to the fact that the 
construction of Round slings is such that only the outer cover can be examined; Round Slings shipment 
offshore use shall be restricted to special loads where the use of Flat Webbing Slings is unsafe due to 
the load shape. 
Note-3: Drilling Equipment like coated tubulars cannot be bundled by Wire Rope Slings; instead they 
can be lifted with Synthetic Slings; however Company Lifting Engineer shall verify the suitability in

WIRE ROPE SLINGS 
 GENERAL 
Wire rope is manufactured from individual steel bars that are 'drawn' to size through a series of dies, 
reducing the diameter of the bar each time until a wire of the desired diameter is obtained.  Individual 
wires are then formed and wound into strands.  The strands are then wound together around a central 
core to form the rope. The core of the rope can be of fiber (FC) or steel (IWRC). 
The number of wires in a strand and the number of strands in a rope are known as the 'construction' of 
the rope.  There are a variety of arrangements in wire rope construction; designs are produced to give 
special characteristics such as flexibility, rotation resistant, crush resistant, etc. 
Wire rope slings are manufactured in accordance with BS EN 13414-1, from wire rope, which meets the 
requirements of BS EN 12385-4, or an equivalent International Standard. Wire rope slings are the most 
commonly used lifting accessory and require care in handling and attention in use. They shall be 
protected from severe wear, abrasion, impact loading, crushing and kinking or similar abuse. Wire rope 
slings shall be adequately protected from crushing and chafing damage by the use of adequate packing. 
The most common types of wire rope sling could be single leg, two legs, three legs, four legs, five legs, 
grommet slings (Round Slings) and flat type wire rope slings. 
 PERIODICITY  
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12. 
 
 THOROUGH EXAMINATION

HMPE (HIGH MODULES POLYETHYLENE) ROPES (DYNEEMA) 
 GENERAL 
HMPE (Dyneema) Ropes shall only be used for Mooring Winches and Inlet Screen Winches designed 
in compliance with BS EN 14492 and shall not be used for Lifting Slings; however Heavy Engineered 
Lifting Operation Slings are accepted to be used in ADNOC Projects provided that the manufacturer 
and manufacturing process shall be in full compliance to this section.  
HMPE (Dyneema) Ropes shall only be used for Mooring, Inlet Screen Winches and Engineered 
Onshore and Offshore lifts Slings. These ropes shall be designed, manufactured, tested and certified in 
accordance with DNVGL OS-E303 or equivalent with minimum Factor of Safety (7:1) for Inlet Screen 
Winch while minimum Factor of Safety (4:1) for Mooring Winches and Engineered onshore/offshore lifts 
Slings. 
The available ISO standards (such as, but not limited to, ISO 14909 & 18264) shall form the basis for 
applicable testing prior to delivery (Strength & Stiffness, linear density, cyclic loading endurance, 
particle ingress resistance, creep property test, core tenacity test, torque properties test …etc.) and 
shall be carried out by the manufacturer and Certification by the Classification Society; however in-
service Inspection can be carried out by Certification Body also. 
The Minimum tenacity of the rope core shall be 1.3 N/tex measured in accordance to Annex B of ISO 
14909. 
The fiber used in the core of the rope shall be high-tenacity HMPE, with an average tenacity of not less 
than 2,5 N/ tex and in accordance with Annex A of ISO 14909. 
Reference shall be made to Appendix 2 of this document regarding the required information to be given 
to the supplier and the required documents to be supplied by the manufacturer. 
The tension applied on the selected sample (As part of the required Strength & Stiffness Rope Tests) 
shall be 50% of MBS (Minimum Breaking Strength) at a rate of 10% MBS per minute and held for 30 
minutes. 
 PERIODICITY  
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12. 
 
 GENERAL CONSIDERATIONS FOR THE USER 
The Rope Manufacturer shall train the purchaser personnel (users) on the of HMPE (Dyneema) Ropes. 
The following shall be avoided:

THOROUGH EXAMINATION 
The following requirements shall be adhered for Billy Pugh carrier: 
 
(a) 
The Billy Pugh carrier shall have valid certificate of inspection from an ADNOC approved 
Third Party using approved Lifting Equipment Inspector and a Unique Identification 
Number/Asset Number, maximum number of persons that can be carried and the maximum 
load that can be carried shall be marked;  
(b) 
Check for any damage or defect on all parts of the unit;  
(c) 
Visually inspect safety load line, four-part sling and all hardware (paying close attention to 
broken wires, flattened sections, crimps, rust …etc.);  
(d) 
Inspect fabric covered stabilizer for tears or excessive wear. Replace inner rubbers if 
damaged, broken, or have lost elasticity;  
(e) 
Inspect vertical rigging ropes and the nuts and bolts that hold them for sufficient and 
consistent tension;  
(f) 
Inspect top and bottom of powder coated aluminium frame including top and bottom ring, 
expanded metal top and center pole. Look for excessive wear, cracks, or corrosion;  
(g) 
Turn center pole (at least 3 full turns) loosening and then tightening. Swab the threads with 
lubricant to prevent seizing;  
(h) 
Inspect top and bottom outer flotation ring covers for damage;  
(i) 
Inspect the rubber feet on the bottom frame for deterioration, damage, or missing feet;  
(j) 
Inspect for modifications or Non-OEM supplied components. Non-OEM components or 
modifications shall be removed;  
(k) 
Defective or worn parts shall be replaced by competent personnel as required;  
(l) 
Inspect the pad eyes on the upper ring of the X-904. There shall be no cracks or damage to 
the welds. The hole in the pad eye should still be round and not elongated by more than 5%. 
The angle of the pad eye from the upper ring should be 20 degrees inside of vertical. Some

A functional examination shall be conducted without load to ensure correct operation of all motions & all 
motion limiting devices such as: Over hoist limit switch, Travel limit switch, Lower anti-collision switch, 
Slack rope limit switch, Anti inclination limit switch, Telescopic mechanism limit switches. 
 
 
 LOAD TEST 
Before and after any load testing the BMU shall be thoroughly examined. The objective of load testing is 
to determine whether the BMU is stable, structurally sound and fit for the use for which it was designed. 
The test program drawn up by the competent person should include every load-bearing part. The 
competent person should determine whether the original or if appropriate the previous test certificate is 
satisfactory, and subsequent testing should be based on this with any necessary modifications to suit the 
requirements of the particular item of equipment. Equipment shall be loaded with its rated load (as per 
manufacture instruction) and subjected to all functions as per manufacturer manual to prove the 
satisfactory working of the machine including the efficiency of the brake (including travel if permitted). 
 
 
SECONDARY BRAKES TEST  
The operation of the secondary brakes shall be checked. 
 
PROOF LOAD TEST  
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix In Appendix 12. 
 
In addition to that during the proof load tests the overload limit switch, overload indicator and the brakes 
shall be observed and checked. After being tested, each function together with all critical accessories is 
to be examined to see whether any part has been damaged or permanently deformed by the load tests. 
Note: The competent person might wish to consult the manufacturer or other design authority to ensure 
that the selected test program is adequate. 
 
 
POST-TEST INSPECTION            
Upon completion of the test a thorough inspection followed by a functional examination as outlined above 
shall be carried out to ensure that the equipment has sustained the overload without any permanent 
damage/injury at the load bearing members & that there are no cracks, permanent deformations, paint 
flaking, loosening of or damage to the structural connections. Suspected areas or welds shall be 
subjected to NDT to ensure no defect. BMU’s functions shall be checked to ensure integrity of all systems 
and proper action of safety devices (Pressure relief valves, or other safety devices) shall be restored and 
verified.

PERSONNEL & GOODS LIFT   
                          
 
Typical Passenger Elevators

## Page 189

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations 
Lifting & Lifted Equipment for Personnel lifting  
APPENDIX 6 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 189 of 301 
 
PERIODICITY  
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix In Appendix 12. 
 
 
THOROUGH EXAMINATION 
The following requirements shall be adhered for personnel and goods lift: 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
FUNCTIONAL TEST 
A functional examination shall be conducted without load to ensure correct operation of all motions & all 
safety devices such as: door switches, Emergency exit door switch, car upper and lower limit switch, car 
buffer limit switches, Pit emergency shut off switch, over speed governor trip switch, slack rope limit switch, 
car/counter weight safety switch, Emergency landing system, emergency calling system, manual lowering 
system and communication system. 
 
 
LOAD TEST 
The objective of load testing is to determine whether the lift is stable, structurally sound and fit for the 
use for which it was designed. The test program drawn up by the competent person should include every 
load-bearing part. The competent person should determine whether the original or if appropriate the 
previous test certificate is satisfactory, and subsequent testing should be based on this with any 
necessary modifications to suit the requirements of the particular item of equipment. The lift should be 
tested with SWL. Calibrated test weights corresponding to the safe working load are to be evenly 
distributed inside. The lift’s car (cabin) and the stability of the rails and the car’s levelling should be 
checked thoroughly. After conducting the Static Load Test, the car is to be moved downward to the lowest 
level to conduct the Dynamic Load Test and stability of rails, cabin and the brakes are to be checked.

## Page 190

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations 
Lifting & Lifted Equipment for Personnel lifting  
APPENDIX 6 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 190 of 301 
 
PROOF LOAD TEST  
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix In Appendix 12. 
In addition to that measure and record the following operations data at no load, 50% of rated load & 100% 
of the rated Load: 
Lift motor speed up and down, Lift motor electrical current up and down. During the proof load tests the 
overload limit switch, overload indicator and the brakes which shall be observed and checked. After being 
tested, lift together with all critical accessories, is to be examined to see whether any part has been 
damaged or permanently deformed by the load tests. 
 
 
OVER SPEED GOVERNOR TEST  
The tripping speed of the governor shall be checked in the direction corresponding to the descent of the 
car or the counterweight. The operation of the stopping control shall be checked in both directions of 
movement. 
 
 
CAR SAFETY GEAR  
The test shall be made while the car is descending, with the required load uniformly distributed over the 
car area, with the machine running until the ropes slip or become slack, and under the following 
conditions: 
The car shall be loaded with the rated load, and travel at rated speed in case of instantaneous safety 
gear or instantaneous safety gear with buffered effect. 
The car shall be loaded with 125% of the rated load, and travel at rated speed in case of progressive 
safety gear.  After the test, it shall be ascertained that no deterioration occurred. 
 
 
COUNTERWEIGHT OR BALANCING WEIGHT SAFETY GEAR:  
The test shall be made while the counterweight is descending, with the machine running until the ropes 
slip or become slack, and under the following conditions: 
The test shall be made with the empty car at rated speed in case of instantaneous safety gear or 
instantaneous safety gear with buffered effect. 
Test shall be made with the empty car at rated speed or lower in case of progressive safety gear. After 
the test, it shall be ascertained that no deterioration occurred. 
 
 
BUFFERS 
 
The test shall be carried out in the following manner: The car with its rated load shall be placed on 
the buffer(s), the rope shall be made slack and it shall be checked that the compression 
corresponds to the figures given in the technical dossier. 
(b) 
Energy accumulation type buffers  with buffered return movement and energy dissipation type 
buffers:  
The car with its rated load and the counterweight shall be brought into contact with the buffers at 
the rated speed. After the test, it shall be ascertained that no deterioration occurred. 
NOTE: The competent person might wish to consult the manufacturer or other design authority to 
ensure that the selected test program is adequate. 
 
 
TESTING EQUIPMENT 
Test weights, Tachometer, Clamp Meter, Megger Tester, shall be supplied by the installer.

PROCEDURE FOR TRANSFER OF PERSONNEL BETWEEN INSTALLATION AND VESSEL 
The Site or Installation Manager and Vessel Captain shall approve the transfer of personnel between the 
installation and vessel. 
For this type of transfer, the following requirements shall be adhered: 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
LIFTING PERSONNEL-HANDLING OF MAN OVERBOARD BOAT WITH OFFSHORE 
CRANE 
When using the offshore crane to handle the MOB, the crane, boat and pennant line shall be approved 
for such use. The approval shall refer to the relevant crane and the additional dynamic load applied by 
the crane and pennant line on the boat. A specific Lifting Plan shall be prepared for handling the MOB 
with the offshore crane. The Lifting Plan shall include, among other things: 
 
 
 
During exercises, the MOB shall only be launched in wind speeds below 10 m/s (20 knots) and 
significant wave height below 1.2 m or allowable movements for the installation. 
 
 
PROOF LOAD TEST  
Refer to Inspection Legislation Matrix in Appendix 12.

flaking, loosening of or damage to the structural connections. Suspected areas or welds shall be 
subjected to NDT to ensure no defect.  Davit launching functions shall be checked to ensure integrity of

restored and verified. 
 
 
LIFTING PERSONNEL - HANDLING OF MAN OVERBOARD BOAT (MOB) WITH DAVIT 
A Lifting Operation Plan (LOP) shall be prepared for handling the MOB with the davit. The LOP shall 
include, among other things: 
 
 
During exercises, the MOB shall only be launched in wind speeds below 10 m/ s (20 knots) and significant 
wave height below 1.2 m or allowable movements for the installation. Each installation shall define limit 
values for maximum allowable movements.

## Page 235

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations 
 
RCI/SLI/LMI 
APPENDIX 8 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 235 of 301 
 
 
RATED CAPACITY INDICATOR/SAFE LOAD INDICATOR/LOAD MOMENT 
INDICATOR 
The crane shall be fitted with an RCI/SLI/LMI giving details of the actual/permissible loads (at the 
appropriate radius) and moments over the working area where the load is lifted or set down. Selecting 
the safe working load and number of falls of rope at the hook block depends on the operating radius (and 
prevailing sea state for offshore cranes). This information shall be readily available to the operator. For 
telescopic boom cranes, the RCI/SLI/LMI shall be capable of automatically resetting the appropriate duty 
ratings applicable to any change of the boom length. 
 
 
VERIFICATION 
The accuracy of the device requires to be verified by the TPIA engineer at the time of inspection, and if it 
is not within the limits, calibration shall be done. 
 
 
CALIBRATION 
Cranes’ RCI/SLI shall be verified by an approved Third Party Company using approved Lifting Equipment 
Inspector on annual basis and every 6 months for “Personnel Lifting Cranes” and to be in compliance 
with BS 7262 with maximum acceptable limit +/- 5% (Onshore Cranes) and to be in compliance with BS 
EN 13852 with maximum acceptable limit +/- 2.5% (Steady platform Offshore Cranes) and +/- 5% for 
floating cranes or as per OEM.  
  
The device shall be calibrated when it exceeds the accuracy of ± 5% (Onshore Cranes) and +/- 2.5% 
(Steady platform Offshore Cranes) and +/- 5% for floating cranes or as per OEM, engaging either OEM 
or competent body. 
 
 
WARNING OF APPROACH TO SWL 
The RCI/SLI/LMI shall give a clear and continuous warning of approach to SWL. The warning shall 
commence at not less than 90 % of the SWL and at not more than 97.5 % of the SWL. The approach to 
SWL warning shall continue to function until the percentage of the load to the SWL falls to some value 
which is less than the value at which the warning was initiated. 
 
 
 WARNING OF OVERLOAD  
The RCI/SLI/LMI shall give a clear and continuous warning of overload. The warning shall commence at 
not less than 102.5 % of the SWL and at not more than 110 % of the SWL. The overload warning shall 
continue to function until the percentage of the load to the SWL falls to some value which is less than the 
value at which the warning was initiated. 
 
 
FORM OF WARNINGS 
The warnings for both approach to SWL and overload shall be continuous and shall be audible and visual 
in accordance with the below table. The audible warnings for approach to SWL and overload shall be 
clearly distinguishable from each other and also from other relevant sounds such as telephones. The 
visual warnings shall be clearly distinguishable under all conditions.
