## Page 237

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations  
Lifting Plan Dossier 
APPENDIX 9 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 237 of 301 
 
 
A Lifting Plan Dossier shall be prepared prior to a complex lift operation. The following shall be a part of 
the dossier as identified by the Approving Authority based on the criticality of the operation as indicated in 
the risk assessment:  
(a) 
Plan and Elevation Drawings into Scale for the Crane and Load Initial and Final Position  
(b) 
Lifting Data Table (refer to Appendix 11) to be available either in the same above mentioned 
Drawing or as separate document.  
(c) 
Rigging Plan Drawing into Scale showing each Sling’s Leg Tension. 
(d) 
Lifting Data Table showing types of Lifting Accessories with their Safe Working Load and Quantity 
to be available either in the same above mentioned Drawing or separate document.  
(e) 
Approved Lift Analysis to show the engineering Calculation of each Sling Leg including the Shackles 
and Spreader Bar/Beam. 
(f) 
Approved Engineering Calculations on the suitability of non-standard Pad-eyes or Trunnions; 
selected Locations to wrap the Sling on the Load and existing structure to be used to suspend the 
installed load. 
(g) 
Site Structural Engineer Inspection Report for the existing structure/transit/lay down area condition 
(If any).  
(h) 
Lifting Method Statement. 
(i) 
Sequence of Operation to perform the task safely.  
(j) 
Contingency Plan (Plan B) should the Crane break-down. 
(k) 
A copy of Crane’s Load Chart. 
(l) 
Crane’s/Rigging/Spreader Bar Valid Third Party Certificate from an ADNOC Group approved Third 
Party Lifting Equipment Inspector working in approved TPA in UAE.  
(m) 
All Training Certificates for the Lifting Team (Lifting Supervisor, Crane Operator and 
Riggers/Banksman) from ADNOC Group Approved Tutor of approved Training Company in UAE.  
For Onshore Mobile Cranes, below points to be considered in addition to the above: 
(a) 
Calculations to select the Appropriate Mat Size to be provided and to specify the Mat’s Material 
(Wooden, Steel or Concrete). 
(b) 
Site Ultimate Ground Bearing Pressure report to be provided for review. 
(c) 
All Four Outriggers/Crawler Tracks Pressure on Ground (under Worst Case Scenario) to be 
calculated using Crane Manufactures Software or Manually using the method specified in UK NCC 
(National Construction College), i.e., [(Crane Weight + Additional Counterweight) x 0.75 + Load 
Weight]/Outrigger Area. 
(d) 
Ultimate Ground Bearing Pressure should be more than Outrigger/Crawler Tracks Pressure on 
Ground after adding a Minimum Safety Factor of 1.5 in case using Crane’s Manufacture’s Software 
(The factor of 1.5 is the absolute minimum, shall be used only under the guidance of an experienced 
geotechnical engineer) and a Minimum Safety Factor of 2 in case of Manual Calculation. 
(e) 
Ground Foundation type to be mentioned. 
(f) 
The Mat can be calculated using the method specified in UK NCC (National Construction College) 
“[(Crane Weight + Additional Counter Weight) x 0.75 + Load Weight]/ Ultimate Ground Bearing 
Pressure. Then lift a load equal to Counterweight and swing it over each Outrigger to check. If it is 
sinks, then a larger Mat to be used. 
(g) 
Safe Distance between the outriggers/Crawler tracks and the end of the jetty to be provided and 
approved by Structural Engineer.

ADNOC Group Lifting Plan: Routine Lifts (Contd.) 
Note1: Reference shall be made to Lifting Data Tables which are required for all Lifts Categories (Refer to Appendix 11). 
Note2: Ground Condition-Temporary works design in place and checked off where appropriate. Compaction Test shall be carried 
out by Soil Specialist 
Note3: where operating jib height equals or exceeds 10m above surrounding structures at any time, the appointed person must 
advise and consult with the manager from any airport/ airfield within 6 km of the site.   
The following non-exhaustive list should be considered during the lift plan along with any other factors that could affect any aspect 
of the lift: 
   Cultural, Communication & Language Difficulties 
   Environmental conditions including weather and 
permissible limits 
   Lighting in the pick-up and Lay-down areas 
   Proximity hazards, obstructions, path of load etc. 
   Working under suspended loads 
   Overturning/ load integrity/ need for tag lines 
   Access and emergency escape routes 
   Suitability and condition of lifting equipment to be used 
   Experience, competence and training of personnel 
   Initial and final load positions and how it will get there 
   Pre-use inspection of equipment by operator 
   Visibility of the load 
   Conflicting tasks in area 
Lift Team

Note 1: Lifting Support Steelwork such as Runway Beams, Lifting Beams, Lifting Frames, Pad-eyes …etc. with no Moving Parts shall have their Periodicity extended to 12 months as long as: 
 
They are not used for supporting personnel, their use is infrequent to the extent that wear is not an adverse factor& are adequately coated/protected so that Corrosion is not an adverse factor. 
*  
these standards have been withdrawn however ADNOC & Contractors still have lifting equipment’s designed to the mentioned standards which shall be inspected and tested accordingly. 
** 
Note 2: Skid Mounted Equipment such as Air Compressors, Generator, Welding Machines, Separators, Motors, Pumps and Tanks. However, skids which are meant for permanent installations and not 
designated as  (POU)/ CCUs can be load tested for self/ design loads subject to meeting the following conditions: 
 
(a) The skids are designed as per Company Skid specification A0-ENG-S-SP-004 and International codes and standards referred therein. The corresponding design calculations and detailing of structural 
framing, lift points/ pad-eyes reviewed and approved by Company Structural Engineer. 
 
(b) The material and fabrication of steel work are in accordance with Company specifications and inspected by Company QA/QC engineer. 
*** 
Note 3: Wire Rope for general purpose, on passenger lifts, on diving operation, Full Body Harness and man-riding winches with Minimum FOS = 10:1 & for Slings with Minimum FOS = 5:1. 
****  Note 4: Minimum Factor of Safety “FOS” = 4:1. 
●: Thorough Examination (Statutory Requirements) including Proof Load Test, Visual, Functional, Performance and NDT “where applicable”. 
 □: Visual Examination Only. 
■: CCU and Skid Mounted Equipment to BS 7072 Proof Load Test = 2 x Maximum Gross Weight. 
■: CCU and Skid Mounted Equipment to BS EN 12079, DNVGL-ST-E271, ABS Guide for Offshore Containers and BS EN ISO 10855 Proof Load Test = 1.5 x Maximum Gross Weight “2 Points Lift”, 2.5 x 
Maximum Gross Weight “4 Points Lift” and vertical impact test either Drop Test or Lowering test with an internal load equal to Payload shall be carried out only on initial certification (When New or after 
substantial repair/alteration) by an ADNOC approved Lifting Equipment Inspector.  
■: Units certified to DNVGL-ST-E272 / ABS PAM Guide shall also be approved and certified by using DNVGL-ST-E271 or DNVGL-ST-E273 or ABS Guide for Offshore Containers.       
■: Portable offshore units (POU’s) to be designed to DNVGL-ST-E273 April 2016 and inspected & Proof Load Tested as per section 5 of the mentioned standard. 
►: CCU Visual Examination every 6 months.

## Page 254

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations  
Lifting Equipment Inspection Legislation Matrix 
APPENDIX 12 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 254 of 301 
◘: NDT (MPI) to be carried out following Proof Load Test ”Initial Inspection” and at intervals as mentioned in the Matrix or as per the ADNOC approved Lifting Equipment Inspector’s discretion. 
₲: No deflection during testing shall be greater than 1/300 of the span of the member (When New or after substantial repair/alteration). Offshore container shall show no permanent deformation or other damage 
after testing. 
∏:  As BS 7072 is obsolete but still in use the following requirements shall be followed: 
- 
These CCU’s shall not be used for transportation of dangerous goods. 
- 
Visual inspection shall be carried out every 6 months.  
- 
Proof load tested to (2 x MGW) shall be carried out every 4 years and after substantial repair. 
- 
NDT shall be carried out every 2 years, after proof load test and after substantial repair. 
- 
Pad eye design and sling set shall meet the minimum requirements of BS EN ISO 10855 or DNVGL-ST-271 or ABS Guide for Offshore containers. 
▲: ILO Table for Beam Clamps, Plate Clamps, Hooks “except Rams-horn”, Swivels, Shackles and Master Links “WLL above 25 t” (Proof Load = WLL x 1.22 + 20 t). 
      ILO Table for Multi Pulley Block including Crane Hook Block “WLL 26 t up to and including 160 t” (Proof Load = WLL x 0.933 + 27 t). 
      ILO Table for Rams-horn Hooks “WLL 51 t to 100 t” (Proof Load = WLL + 50 t). 
      ILO Table for Lifting Beams/Frames “WLL 11 t up to and including 160 t” (Proof Load = WLL x 1.04 + 9.6 t). 
▼: Hydraulic/Manual Jacks Type Tests (Static Test = 1.5 x WLL and Dynamic Test = 1.25 x WLL) and In-service Test (1.1 x WLL). 
◄: Chain Block, Lever Hoist, Tirfor, Trolley, Manual Winch & Single/Multi Pulley Block (Static Test=1.5xWLL & 1.25xWLL & Dynamic Test=1.1xWLL) and In-service (1xWLL) every 12 months. 
₭: Construction hoists (Material lifting) shall be proof load tested to 1.25% WLL (Proof Load Test) and 1 x WLL (Drop Test) on completion of the hoist installation or alteration/modification, 1 x WLL for (In-service 
hoists) every   12 months along with verification of the overload cut-off and NDT. Construction hoists (Personnel Lifting) shall be proof load tested to 1.25% WLL (Proof Load Test) and 1 x WLL (Drop Test) 
on completion of the hoist  Installation or alteration/modification, 1 x WLL for (In-service hoists) every 6 months along with verification of the overload cut-off and NDT. 
♦:   Initial Factory Test including any required Type Test as per the relevant Standard including provision of Manufacturer’s Certificate of Conformity. 
♣:  Shall be calibrated annually. 
β:  Sample to be destructed to verify materials properties. 
♥: New Webbing Sling to be Proof Load Tested 2xWLL. Metal end fittings Proof Load Test only with Webbing Sling Factor of Safety Minimum=7:1 and Metal Ends Minimum Factor of Safety=5:1. 
×:  In Periodic/In-service Inspection & Certification, Load Test with 100% SWL is required; however Proof Load Test might be carried out as per the discretion of the Inspection Body. 
π:  Plate Clamps Type Test = 3 x WLL on Mechanical Load Bearing Parts. 
θ:  Pneumatic/Hydraulic, Electric/Mechanical Winches (Static Test=1.25 x WLL and Dynamic Test=1.1 x WLL) and In-service Test (1xWLL) every 12 months including Overload Limiter Verification. 
Λ:  Lifeboat/Life Raft/Rescue Boat Davits shall be functionally tested with SWL i.e. Lifeboat Weight every 6 months, In-service Winch Dynamic Proof Load Test 1.1xSWL “of each Davit” every 60 months. 
Ψ: Lifeboat/Life Raft/Rescue Boat Davits shall be Proof Load Tested (2.2 x Maximum Working Load) after installation & following modification, In-service Static Winch Proof Load Test 1.5xSWL every 60 months. 
μ: Davits shall be Proof Load Test to 125% SWL, associated Manual Lifting Appliances shall be proof load tested separately to 150% SWL and Powered Lifting Appliances to 125% SWL. 
Ω: Asymmetric Wedge & Socket for Crane Wire Rope Termination shall be Type Tested to a load of: 1) 20% of the Rope Minimum Breaking Force for 2 Minutes. 2) 60% of the Rope Minimum Breaking Force then 
increasing this  load at a rate of note more than 0.5% of the Breaking Force per Second “the test shall be continued until either rope slip or breakage occurred”. 
∑: Master Link in 2-leg Chain Slings shall be Proof Load Tested to 3.5xWLL of Chain, Intermediate Links in 3/4-leg Chain Slings to 4xWLL of Chain, Master Link in 3/4-leg Chain Sling to 5.25xWLL. 
σ: Master Link “to 25 t” in 2-leg Chain Slings to be Proof Load Tested to 2.8xWLL of Chain, Intermediate Links in 3/4-leg Chain Slings to 3.2xWLL, Master Link in 3/4-leg C. Sling to 4.2xWLL. 
£: Man-Basket shall be Proof Load Tested to 3 x M.G.W. with 4 Points-lift, NDT following Proof Load Test and every 6 months and shall be visually checked by the user before each use. 
¥: Allowable Deflection at SWL for Overhead Crane=Span Length/750, Gantry Crane to BS 5950-1 (BS EN 1993-6) = Span Length/600 & Gantry Crane to BS 449-2 (BS EN 1993-6) = Span Length/360.  
€: Slewing Jib Crane allowable Deflection with Classification of A4 to A6 ”Medium Duty” at SWL = R/250 “R is the Effective Radius”. 
†: Slewing Jib Crane to BS EN 14985 including “Luffing”: Dynamic Proof Load Test = 1.1 x SWL and Static Proof Load Test = 1.5 x SWL. 
‡: Runway Beam allowable Deflection at SWL = Length of Span/500 for Simply Supported Beam” and = Length of Span/250 for Cantilever Beam. 
₸: Mobile Gantry A-Frame allowable Deflection at SWL = Length of Span/500. 
∆: Suspended Cradle “BMU” Type Tests (Static Test = 1.5 x WLL and Dynamic Test = 1.25 x WLL) and In-service Test (1 x SWL) including Overload Limit Switch Verification. 
☼: Fall Arrestor Dynamic & Static Test as per BS EN 353/BS EN 364. 
○: Man-Basket (Transfer) “FROG” shall be load tested as per the Manufacturer’s Instructions. 
∞: Skates and Ground Trolley Dynamic Load Test = 1.1 x SWL and Static Load Test = 1.5 x SWL. 
¶: LARS Equipment shall be tested as per IMCA D 018 Detailed Sheets # 3, 4, 22.1, 22.2, 23, 29.1, 29.2, 30, 31, 32.1, 32.2 and 35.

©: Gin Wheel to be 100% SWL Load Tested every 6 months and 4 x SWL Proof Load Tested every 4 years. 
Ð: Gangway designed to BS EN 526 shall be Proof Load Test 2 x SWL with Load on Mid-span and Deflection shall not exceed 2% of the Length. Gangway designed to BS MA 78 shall be Proof Load Tested 2 x 
SWL with Uniformly distributed load and Deflection shall not exceed Length/75 and shall have 4 Standard Pad-eyes directed diagonally to COG and Proof Load Test 2 x Tare Weight every 4 years. All Gangways

¤:  HMPE Dyneema Ropes shall only be used for Mooring/Inlet Screen Winches and engineered lifts slings; reference shall be made to Appendix 2 ; Section 4.21 for the required type approvals for the yarn 
and rope that shall be carried out by the manufacturer and Certification by the Classification Society; however in-service Inspection can be carried out by Certification Body also. Minimum Factor of Safety 
(7:1) for Inlet Screen Winch while minimum Factor of Safety (4:1) for Mooring Winches and Engineered onshore/ offshore lifts sling shall only be considered.

## Page 256

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations  
Lifting Team and Training Companies Requirements 
 
APPENDIX 13 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 256 of 301 
 
A program of training appropriate to each Crane/Forklift/Man-lift discipline shall be provided by an ADNOC 
approved Training Company, experienced and qualified tutors to provide instruction in performance, 
maintenance, inspection and safe operation of the lifting equipment and its associated components. 
 
The system of assessment by an ADNOC approved Training Company using approved Tutors/Assessors valid 
for 2 years should enable continual and effective measurement of the theoretical and practical performance of 
Lifting Team personnel; 3 stages assessment for Crane Operators implemented in some training companies 
is also accepted however; it will not supersede the minimum 3-day training course as per below Table.  
 
Certification and revalidation of certificates shall be evidenced through re-assessment of personnel by an 
ADNOC approved Training Company using approved Tutors/Assessors on theoretical and practical issues of 
safe use of relevant equipment specifically to the particular disciplines of personnel and equipment types. 
 
It is the responsibility of an ADNOC approved Training Company using approved Tutors/Assessor to carry out 
assessment for only personnel who attended and successfully completed Full Training Course from ADNOC 
approved Training Company. 
 
 
In order to approve the company as an ADNOC approved Training Company, the following are the 
minimum requirements: 
 
The Company shall be qualified as an ADNOC approved Company for performing Technical 
Training Services and their personnel to have hands-on experience for Lifting Operations 
including Third Party Inspection Companies; 
 
The Company shall submit their proposed Training Materials to ADNOC for approval; 
 
Company shall submit the CV’s of their proposed tutors as part of the submitted documents; 
 
Dry run course shall be carried out by the tutor for further ADNOC approval on tutor and 
training materials; and  
 
ADNOC will issue and update regularly the list of approved Companies and Tutors. 
 
 
Bachelor/Diploma in Mechanical engineering or equivalent; 
 
Minimum 10 years of experience related to lifting equipment inspection and training, such as 
crane and forklift operator and riggers training; 
 
Familiar with international lifting equipment standards and codes for inspection and 
certification; 
 
Shall hold certification in LEEA (Entry Exam, Lifting Machines Manual, Lifting Gear General, 
Lifting Machines Power and Runway & Light Crane Structure (or equivalent); 
 
Approved from ADNOC Group for relevant Lifting Training Course; and  
 
The minimum required number of Training & Assessment days relevant to each course 
program including the validity of Training Certificates will be given in Table 9 in below.

## Page 258

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations  
Lifting Team and Training Companies Requirements 
 
APPENDIX 13 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 258 of 301 
 
 
All Crane Operators shall receive 3-day Training Course by an ADNOC approved Training 
Company including an assessment and thereafter shall be assessed every 2 years following the 
completion of a refresher course. 
 
All Mobile/Pedestal/Overhead/Tower Crane Operators with less than 5 years of experience shall 
receive One Month Practical Training as a minimum in addition to the 3-day Training Course by 
ADNOC approved Training Company then shall work under the direct supervision of competent 
Crane Operator for not less than 3 months then he shall be assessed by an ADNOC approved 
Training Company prior to start Crane Operation. 
 
All Forklift/Man-lift/BMU-Cradle Operators with less than 1 year experience shall receive Two 
Weeks Practical Training as a minimum in addition to the 2-day Training Course by ADNOC 
approved Training Company then shall work under the direct supervision of competent 
Forklift/Man-lift Operator for not less than 1 month then he shall be assessed by ADNOC 
approved Training Company prior to start Forklift/Man-lift Operation. 
 
All Riggers and Banksman with less than 6 months experience shall receive 6 Weeks Practical 
Training as a minimum in addition to the 2-day Training Course (Rigger) and 1-day Training 
Course (Banksman) by an ADNOC approved Training Company then shall work under the direct 
supervision of competent Rigger/Banksman for not less than 6 months then he shall be assessed 
by an ADNOC approved Training Company prior to start Riggers/Banksman Duties. 
 
All the Roles in above Table shall attend the Assessment after successfully completing the 
relevant Training Course, Assessment without Training Records from ADNOC approved Training 
Company shall be avoided. 
 
The employer of the Lifting Team personnel shall bear the cost of all trainings and assessments 
 
 
All Training Companies shall follow the minimum Training Courses Syllabus as per the 
requirement of ADNOC Group; however, the Training Company can include additional Syllabus 
as necessary. 
 
All Course Materials will be reviewed and evaluated by Lifting Technical Authority during the Dry-
Run as part of ADNOC Approval on the Training Company including Tutors. 
 
ADNOC is currently developing CDM (Course Design Manual) for the all Lifting Technical 
Training Courses. CDM will have all the elements of the course outlines that any New Training 
Provider shall comply with it. A sufficient Grace Period shall be given to ADNOC approved 
Training Providers to comply with CDM in addition to the Courses Syllabuses listed below; any 
deviation from CDM shall be reviewed and approved by Lifting Technical Authority.

## Page 275

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations  
Pre-Use Checklists for Lifting Appliances and Accessories 
 
APPENDIX 14 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1

Page 275 of 301 
 
Pre-Use Check List for Powered Chain Hoist: 
No. 
POWERED CHAIN HOIST CHECK LIST 
Satisfactory 
(Yes/ No) 
Remarks 
1 
Ensure Powered Chain Hoist is clearly marked with a unique 
Identification Number, SWL, and Color Code and have a valid 
Certificate of Inspection from an approved TPIA. 
 
 
2 
Examine side plates and check for wear, distortion and cracks. 
 
 
 
3 
Examine wheels, axles, bearings, wheel tread and flanges. 
 
 
 
4 
If geared travel, check gear teeth on wheel flanges and hand 
chain wheel shaft for alignment, broken teeth etc. 
 
 
5 
Examine electrical pendent and the cable seats properly in the 
pendent sockets. 
 
 
6 
Check the chain guides are not deformed and are correctly 
positioned to ensure smooth feed of chain onto assembly. 
 
 
7 
Examine Overload clutch/bars and check for deformation. 
 
 
8 
Examine suspension plates/yokes/shackles for wear, distortion 
and cracking. 
 
 
9 
Where an adjusting screwed bar is fitted, check for straightness 
and examine threads for damage and check locking device. 
 
 
10 
Ensure both the female threaded spigots (mounted on the side 
plates) are not damaged or deformed. 
 
 
11 
If fitted with chain hoist check the hoist as per hoist check list. 
 
 
 
12 
Check for power supply cable ant it conditions of insulation, etc. 
 
 
 
13 
Check for all the operating push buttons are operating smoothly. 
 
 
 
14 
Check for all the operating push buttons are operating smoothly. 
 
 
 
Any Other Comments/ Recommendations: 
 
 
Signature:        
 
Date:

                             No: Not Suitable for Service or Repair Required

## Page 276

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations  
Pre-Use Checklists for Lifting Appliances and Accessories 
 
APPENDIX 14 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 276 of 301 
 
Pre-Use Check List for Hand Pallet Truck: 
No. 
HAND PALLET TRUCK CHECK LIST 
Satisfactory 
(Yes/ No) 
Remarks 
1 
Ensure Hand Pallet Truck is clearly marked with a unique 
Identification Number, SWL, Color Code and have a valid 
Certificate of Inspection from an approved TPIA 
 
 
2 
Examine body of cylinder/jack and check for impact damage, 
cracks and oil leaks (include inlet/ outlet couplings). 
 
 
3 
Operate cylinder/ jack, pumping the ram to full stroke. 
 
 
 
4 
Examine the ram and check for belling, scoring and corrosion 
and impact damage. 
 
 
5 
If the ram is threaded externally and fitted with a locking collar, 
examine threads and check for stretch. 
 
 
6 
Examine round seal and check for oil leakage. 
 
 
 
7 
Open valve to lower/release and ensure the ram goes down 
smoothly. 
 
 
8 
Examine all the fittings and ensure they are not perished, cut or 
in any way damaged. 
 
 
9 
With the ram in the lowered position check the oil level of the 
jack/ pump unit. 
 
 
10 
Function test the pump and ensure the valve does not leak when 
closed under pressure. 
 
 
11 
Examine the wheels damage, worn out and proper rotation. 
 
 
 
Any Other Comments/ Recommendations: 
 
 
Signature:          
                                  
Date:

                              No: Not Suitable for Service or Repair Required

## Page 277

Pre-Use Checklists for Lifting Appliances and Accessories 
 
APPENDIX 14 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1

Page 277 of 301 
 
Pre-Use Check List for Runway Beams: 
No. 
RUNWAY BEAMS CHECK LIST 
Satisfactory 
(Yes/ No) 
Remarks 
1 
Ensure Runway Beam is clearly marked with a unique 
Identification Number, SWL, and Color Code and have a valid 
Certificate of Inspection from an approved TPIA. 
 
 
2 
Check for complete welding, cracks, permanent deformation. 
 
 
 
3 
Clean and examine the flange surface for any uneven surface, 
waviness. 
 
 
4 
Examine the joints between the beams is there any uneven 
welding, and the excessive gap between the beams, 
 
 
5 
Examine the joints lindaptor clamps between the beams is there 
any loose on the bolts and nuts. 
 
 
6 
Examine the correct size of the bolts and the torque applied on the 
beams. 
 
 
7 
Examine the end stopper plates welding /mounting bolts, and 
rubber bumper. 
 
 
8 
Examine the top cross member on the beam for bent, permanent 
deformation and paint flaking. 
 
 
9 
If cracks are suspected, carry out N.D.T. 
 
 
 
10 
If N.D.T. has been performed and all is satisfactory, repaint before 
issuing. 
 
 
Any Other Comments/ Recommendations:

Pre-Use Check List for Beam Trolley:

BEAM TROLLEY CHECK LIST 
Satisfactory 
(Yes/ No) 
Remarks 
1 
Ensure Beam Trolley is clearly marked with a unique 
Identification Number, SWL, and Color Code and have a valid 
Certificate of Inspection from an approved TPIA. 
 
 
2 
Examine top and bottom of the runway beam for deformation/ or 
distortion. 
 
 
3 
Check function of the operating chain any crack, twist, bent etc. 
 
 
 
4 
Ensure the anti-tilt device in place and check for any damage. 
 
 
 
5 
Where accessible, examine runner wheel for wear and/ or 
distortion. 
 
 
6 
Examine pinion gear teeth. Check for wear and/ or distortion and 
ensure satisfactory seating of runner wheel teeth. 
 
 
7 
Examine body casing and check for cracks, distortion, missing 
bolts/ screws or any defect, which may affect safe operation. 
 
 
8 
Examine (as far as possible) internal frame side plates. 
 
 
 
9 
Ensure chain guide rollers and chain wheel are in position and 
functioning. 
 
 
10 
Examine the load suspended point /eye and check for distortion/ 
wear. 
 
 
11 
Examine suspend hoist and spin for bent, crack, permanent 
deformation. 
 
 
12 
Examine the end stopper on the runway beam, and ensure it is 
fixed properly. 
 
 
13 
Examine complete length of the runway beam check for stretch, 
deformed/bent damage, and excessive corrosion.

Pre-Use Check List for Manually Operated Chain Blocks:

CHAIN BLOCKS CHECK LIST 
Satisfactory 
(Yes/ No) 
Remarks 
1 
Ensure Manually operated Chain Hoist is clearly marked with a 
unique Identification Number, SWL, Color Code and have a valid 
Certificate of Inspection from an approved TPIA 
 
 
2 
Examine top and bottom hooks and check for stretch and/or 
distortion. 
 
 
3 
Check function of safety catches. 
 
 
 
4 
Ensure hooks swivel free in yokes and are secure in their 
mountings. 
 
 
5 
Where accessible, examine load pin for wear and/or distortion. 
 
 
 
6 
Examine load chain sheaves, check for wear and/or distortion and 
ensure satisfactory seating of load chain. 
 
 
7 
Examine body casing and check for cracks, distortion, missing 
bolts/ screws or any defect, which may affect safe operation. 
 
 
8 
Examine (as far as possible) internal frame side plates. 
 
 
 
9 
Ensure chain guide rollers and chain stripper is in position and 
functioning. 
 
 
10 
Examine live end and dead end load chain anchor pins and check 
for distortion/wear. 
 
 
11 
Suspend hoist and spin hand chain wheel (in hoist mode) to check 
ratchet pawl function-listen for healthy clicking sound. 
 
 
12 
Examine hand chain wheel and chain and ensure it seats correctly 
in the pockets. 
 
 
13 
Examine complete length of load chain and check for stretch, 
deformed/ bent links, nicks, wear, heat damage, weld spatter and 
excessive corrosion.

Pre-Use Check List for Manually Operated Lever Hoists:

LEVER HOISTS CHECK LIST 
Satisfactory 
(Yes/ No) 
Remarks 
1 
Ensure Manually operated Lever Hoist is clearly marked with a 
unique Identification Number, SWL, Color Code and have a valid 
Certificate of Inspection from an approved TPIA. 
 
 
2 
Examine top and bottom hooks and check for stretch and/or 
distortion. 
 
 
3 
Check function of safety catches. 
 
 
 
4 
Ensure hooks swivel free in yokes and are secure in their 
mountings. 
 
 
5 
Where accessible, examine load pin for wear and/or distortion. 
 
 
 
6 
Examine load chain sheaves, check for wear and/or distortion and 
ensure satisfactory seating of load chain. 
 
 
7 
Examine body casing and check for cracks, distortion, missing 
bolts/ screws or any defect, which may affect safe operation. 
 
 
8 
Examine (as far as possible) internal frame side plates. 
 
 
 
9 
Ensure chain guide rollers and chain stripper/ de-twister are in 
position and functioning. 
 
 
10 
Examine load chain "live" end anchor and "dead" end load chain 
stop. (a deformed chain stop can pull through the hoist). 
 
 
11 
Examine the operating lever and ensure it is not deformed. (a bent 
lever could be an indicator of overload). 
 
 
12 
Operate the lever in both hoisting and lowering modes to check 
operation of pawls. (Listen for healthy clicking sound). 
 
 
13 
Examine complete length of load chain and check for stretch, 
deformed/ bent links, nicks, wear, heat damage, weld spatter and 
excessive corrosion.

Remarks

Ensure Jaw Winch (Tirfor) is clearly marked with a unique

2 
Examine load pin/hook and hook seating in the side casings for 
wear, distortion or cracking. 
 
 
3 
Where hooks are fitted, check operation of safety catch and 
ensure the hook is free to swivel. 
 
 
4

Examine casing and check for cracks, dents or distortion, which 
may affect the workings of the internal mechanism. 
 
 
8 
Using a torch, examine the internals through the opening at the 
top of the hoist and ensure it is adequately lubricated. 
 
 
9 
Examine hoist rope ensuring it is the correct one for the hoist and 
that it is not damaged or worn.

Examine body of cylinder/jack and check for impact damage,

Operate cylinder/jack, pumping the ram to full stroke.

Examine the ram and check for belling, scoring and corrosion and

Pre-Use Check List for Wire Rope Pulley Blocks/ Snatch Blocks: 
No. 
WIRE ROPE PULLEY BOLCKS/ SNATCH BLOCK CHECK 
LIST 
Satisfactory 
(Yes/ No) 
Remarks 
1 
Ensure Wire Rope Pulley Block/ Snatch Block is clearly marked 
with a unique Identification Number, SWL, and Color Code and 
have a valid Certificate of Inspection from an approved TPIA. 
 
 
2 
Examine sheaves for wear in the rope groove, cracks or 
distortion. 
 
 
3 
Try to lift sheaves to check bushes for wear. 
 
 
 
4 
Spin the sheaves to check bearings/bushes and ensure 
smoothness of operation. 
 
 
5 
Ensure all grease ports are clean and unblocked and that the 
block is well lubricated. 
 
 
6 
Examine swivel head fitting (including shackle/hook) and check 
for wear/stretch. 
 
 
7 
Examine (if possible) thrust bearing/washer and ensure 
smoothness of operation. 
 
 
8 
Examine upper load pin/spigots and check for wear/distortion. 
 
 
 
9 
If head fitting/hook is of the shank type, check security of shank 
and nut and examine for stretch/distortion. Examine crosshead 
for wear. 
 
 
10 
Examine side plates/straps and check for distortion, wear or 
cracking (especially around load bearing pins). 
 
 
11 
Ensure there are no sharp edges or burrs on the side plates, 
which may be harmful to the wire rope. 
 
 
12 
If fitted, examine becket and check for wear, stretch or cracking. 
 
 
 
13 
Check all spacers and tie bolts and ensure they are not deformed. 
 
 
 
Any Other Comments/ Recommendations: 
 
 
Signature:        
                                    
Date: 
Key: Satisfactory- Yes: Suitable for Service 
                             No: Not Suitable for Service or Repair Required

Ensure Plate Clamp is clearly marked with a unique Identification

Pre-Use Check List For Chain Sling  
No. 
CHAIN SLING CHECK LIST 
Satisfactory 
(Yes/ No) 
Remarks 
1 
Ensure Chain Slings is clearly marked with a unique Identification 
Number, SWL, Color Code and have a valid Certificate of 
Inspection from an approved TPIA. 
 
 
2 
Lay out chain sling on the floor, remove all twists from the legs 
and check for bent/deformed links. 
 
 
3 
With multi-leg slings, match up legs to check for signs of stretch 
in individual legs. 
 
 
4 
Examine the chain legs along their complete length and check for 
wear between links, nicks, twists and elongated links. 
 
 
5 
Check for wear between chain links and load pins. 
 
 
 
6 
Check security of load pins. 
 
 
7 
Check complete sling legs for heat and/or chemical damage. 
 
 
 
8 
Closely examine chain links closest to end fittings as they are 
often abraded due to sling being dragged over rough surfaces. 
 
 
9 
Examine end fittings and check for wear, stretch and twisting. 
 
 
 
10 
If fitted, examine quadruple/quadruple assembly and check for 
wear, corrosion and cracking. 
 
 
11 
Where fitted, check function of safety catches. 
 
 
 
12 
Check for twist, bent, cut, worn out. 
 
 
 
Any Other Comments/ Recommendations: 
 
 
Signature: 
 
Date: 
Key: Satisfactory- Yes: Suitable for Service 
No: Not Suitable for Service or Repair Required

## Page 287

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations  
Pre-Use Checklists for Lifting Appliances and Accessories 
 
APPENDIX 14 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 287 of 301 
 
Pre-Use Check List for Wire Rope Sling: 
No. 
WIRE ROPE SLING CHECK LIST 
Satisfactory 
(Yes/ No) 
Remarks 
1 
Ensure Wire Rope Slings is clearly marked with a unique 
Identification Number, SWL, Color Code and have a valid 
Certificate of Inspection from an approved TPIA 
 
 
2 
Examine each leg along its entire length and check for wear, 
corrosion, abrasion, mechanical damage and broken wires. 
 
 
3 
Measure diameter to determine if there is any reduction from 
original due to wear or stretch (caused by overload). 
 
 
4 
Examine each ferrule and check for impact damage/tearing. 
It shall be free of cracks and other deformities. 
 
 
5 
Check that the end of the eye loop does not terminate inside the 
ferrule unless it is of the longer tapered design. 
 
 
6 
Examine each thimble and check for correct fitting, also check for 
snagging damage caused by sling being dragged. 
 
 
7 
Check for elongation in the eyes, which could indicate 
overloading. 
 
 
8 
Examine wire rope around thimbles as it is often abraded due to 
sling being dragged over rough surfaces. 
 
 
9 
If fitted, examine quadruple/quadruple assembly and check for 
wear, corrosion and cracking. 
 
 
10 
If fitted, check any terminal fittings i.e. hooks, shackles etc. for 
wear, damage etc. 
 
 
11 
Where fitted, check function of safety catches. 
 
 
 
12 
Check for Broken wires, kink, twist, waviness, birdcage, worn out, 
core protrusion, standard protrusion. 
 
 
Any Other Comments/ Recommendations: 
 
 
Signature: 
 
Date: 
Key: Satisfactory- Yes: Suitable for Service 
No: Not Suitable for Service or Repair Required

WEBBING SLING CHECK LIST 
Satisfactory 
(Yes/ No) 
Remarks 
1 
Ensure Webbing Slings is clearly marked with a unique 
Identification Number, SWL, Color Code and have a valid 
Certificate of Inspection from an approved TPIA 
 
 
2 
Ensure the Webbing sling is not color coded with some paint. 
(Plastic Ties shall be used for color coding purpose) 
 
 
3 
Examine the sling along its entire length and check for cuts or 
tears and burst stitching (especially around eyes). 
 
 
4 
Check complete sling for chemical or heat damage. 
 
 
 
5 
If it is a "Round" sling, examine outer sheath and ensure there 
has been no ingress of foreign bodies into load bearing fibers. 
 
 
6 
If metal eyes are fitted, check that they are not worn, distorted or 
cracked. 
 
 
7 
Check for wear in the webbing where it is fitted to the metal eye. 
 
 
 
8 
Check there is no abrasion damage caused by being dragged 
from under a load. 
 
 
9 
Ensure slings are not knotted. 
 
 
 
10 
Check for cuts, teat, burst stitching damage, localized damage. 
 
 
 
11 
Check for oil and chemical attack.

SHACKLES CHECK LIST 
Satisfactory 
(Yes/ No) 
Remarks 
1 
Ensure Shackles is clearly marked with a unique Identification 
Number, SWL, Color Code and have a valid Certificate of

2 
Remove shackle pin and examine for wear, deformation and 
cracking. 
 
 
3 
Ensure it is the right pin for the shackle (i.e. not a higher tensile 
pin in an alloy shackle). 
 
 
4 
Check pin threads and shackle body or nut threads for wear/ 
deformation. 
 
 
5 
Examine shackle body and check for wear in the crown and in 
pin holes, deformation and cracking. 
 
 
6 
In the case of safety pin shackles, ensure split pins are fitted. 
 
 
 
7 
Check for wear and worn out on threads if screw pin shackles. 
 
 
 
8 
Check for wear, stretch, distortion and look for hairline cracks at 
the crown and pin.

1 
Ensure Rigging Screw/Turn Buckles is clearly marked with a

2 
Unscrew both ends of rigging screw to separate the three main

Clean and examine the threads and check for wear, stretch or

Satisfactory

Remarks

Ensure Eye Bolts is clearly marked with a unique Identification

2 
If eyebolts/ eye nuts are previously unused, remove protective

and look for hairline cracks at the crown.

Page 298 of 301 
 
 
 
 
The lift weight shall be calculated or as weighed and include Contingency Factors; 
 
The contingency factor on calculated weight of equipment shall be at least 10% to allow for 
inaccuracies and any internal containment; 
 
The contingency factor on calculated weight of structural steel assemblies shall be at least 
10% to allow for tolerances, paint, welds etc.; 
 
The contingency factor on final measured weight shall be at least 3% to allow for 
inaccuracies in measurement; 
 
The variability in the estimation of center of gravity shall be accounted for by assuming an 
envelope for its location in accordance with the Project specifications; 
 
The length of lifting slings/ grommets shall be such that the tilt of the module when lifted shall 
be less than 2 degrees; and  
 
The hook loads should be calculated as follows: 
 
Static Hook Load (SHL) = Gross weight + Rigging Weight+ Special loads (If any). 
Hook Block weight needs to be added in case the same is not included in the crane 
capacity chart. 
 
Dynamic Hook Load (DHL) = SHL x DAF (Dynamic Amplification Factor). 
 
The minimum DAF shall be as indicated in the Table 1.1 below for a single crane/ 
hook lift provided the lift will not take place in adverse weather conditions as defined 
in DNVGL-ST-N001. 
 
The below Values shall also apply for lifts by 2 Cranes on the same vessel. 
 
For offshore lifts by 2 or more hooks on the same crane boom, total load on the 
crane boom structure shall be documented based on below values multiplied by 1.10 
unless certified crane curves for this specific application can be provided. 
 
DAF is not applicable in Complex Lifts if carried out by Pedestal Crane having 
Dynamic Load Chart generated by the Crane’s Manufacturer as per applicable 
referenced standard i.e. (Pedestal Cranes on Platforms, Jack-up Barges, 
Rigs …etc.). 
 
The above Dynamic Hook Loads are to be checked against crane capacities.

appropriate Software to derive limiting sea states and their directions, The Analysis 
Study shall be approved by IVB. 
 
Inshore DAF factors shall be considered to a lift by a crane vessel to/from a vessel 
or to/ from the jetty or to/ from barge in sheltered waters and to lift from the deck of 
a crane vessel onto a fixed platform at an offshore location. 
 
Inshore DAF factors shall be considered for onshore crawler cranes travelling with 
load in addition to Onshore DAF. 
 
Inshore DAF factors shall be considered to a lift by a mobile crane from/to barge in 
the jetty. 
 
Inshore DAF factors shall be considered to a lift by a crane barge from/to fixed or 
floating structures in the jetty or to/ from the jetty. 
 
Inshore DAF factors shall be considered to a lift by a crane barge from/to fixed 
structures in an offshore location. 
 
Onshore DAF factors are applicable to a lift to/from a vessel moored alongside a 
quay using a land-based crane. If a ship’s crane is used, inshore factors apply. 
 
Onshore DAF factors are applicable to a lift in onshore without travelling the load. 
 
Offshore DAF factors shall be considered to a lift by a crane barge from/to fixed or 
floating structures in an offshore location. 
 
Offshore DAF factors shall be considered to a lift by a crane vessel from another 
vessel to a fixed platform or barge in an offshore location. 
 
If the above DAF cannot be considered in any Complex Lift; including “moving the 
Barge/Vessel with suspended load” then a Motion Analysis Study using appropriate 
Software to derive limiting sea states and their directions to be prepared and 
approved by an IVB.

Note: If the lift design is performed according to clause 2.4.2 e of ADNOC Offshore SP-1030 to account 
for skew load distribution then the above SKL factors need not be considered. 
 
 
 
A minimum yaw factor of 1.05; and 
 
A tilt factor of 1.03 (In case of single hook lift and the tilt is less than 2 Degree, this factor 
need not be considered). 
 
 
The rigging comprising slings and/or grommets and shackles shall be new or in good 
condition and shall have valid certificates; 
 
All slings and grommets shall meet the requirement of International Marine Contractors 
Association guidelines IMCA M 179 – “Guidance on The Use of Cable Laid Slings and 
Grommets”, wherever such requirements are not covered in this document; however, the 
minimum accepted any type of steel rope slings FOS shall not be less than 4; 
 
The Safe Working Load (SWL) or Working Load Limit (WLL) of these components shall be 
as provided by the manufacturer and shall be greater than or equal to the sling design load; 
 
The Sling design load shall be calculated by lifting analysis and shall include all factors 
described in this section including a minimum consequence factor of 1.3; and 
 
If SWL is not provided by the manufacturer, it shall be calculated from the wire rope Minimum 
Breaking Load (MBL). A factor of safety (FOS) shall be applied on the MBL. According to 
working stress design method to derive the SWL. This calculation is written as follows: 
Sling Design load = Dynamic Sling load (Includes consequence factor and other factors) 
SWL ≥ Sling Design load  
SWL = MBL/FS 
FOS shall be according to section 16.4 of DNV GL-ST-N001 (Consequence factor need not be 
considered for calculating the FOS if the same has already been included in the dynamic sling 
load.) 
Alternatively, as per API RP 2A, slings shall be selected to have a FS of 4.0 for the MBL compared 
to the sling load considering COG shift and Skew load effects. 
 
Cradles, access platforms with handrails and other rigging aids shall be provided on the jacket/ topside 
for safe and easy access to install/release slings, grommets and shackle pins as necessary. 
The rigging aid platform shall be designed for quick release and designed to withstand the rigging loads 
plus a uniform live load of 5 kN/ m². 
 
 
The lifting bars/ beams/ frames shall have overall dimensions such that the associated slings/grommets 
do not clash with the object being lifted.

## Page 301

HSE Management System 
Operations Safety Standards 
Lifting and Hoisting Operations  
Factors for Complex Lift 
 
APPENDIX 16 
Standard No.: HSE-OS-ST19 
Version No.: 1 
Effective date: August 2019 
Page 301 of 301 
The design axial forces and bending moments in the elements of these components shall be determined 
for the design lift load as in Section 1.1 of this Appendix above, using the geometry of the rigging 
arrangement to resolve the forces. 
A consequence factor of 1.35 (WLL=1.35 x Sling Tension) shall be applied to the design forces of these 
components and these components shall be designed in accordance with AISC with no increase in the 
allowable stress is permitted. The design forces shall be determined by performing a lifting analysis.  
The Lifting Bars/Beams/Frames shall have valid inspection certificates from an approved Third Party 
using approved Lifting Equipment Inspector. 
Design calculations including lifting analysis and drawings shall be approved by Lifting Technical 
Authority. 
All lifting calculations and drawings shall be approved by an IVB. 
 
 
Lifting loads at the Pad-eyes, Pad-ears and Trunnions shall be calculated in accordance with Section 
1.1 of this Appendix above. All calculations shall be approved by ADNOC Structural Engineer. 
In addition, a lateral load of 5% of the lift load shall be applied at the point of action of the lift load on Pad-
eyes, Pad-ears and Trunnions.  
Also, an additional consequence factor of 1.35 on the lift point loads is applicable for the design of these 
components.  
When the pad-eye is designed in accordance with API factor of 2.0, it includes all factors (consequence 
factor, tilt factor, SKL factor, DAF etc.)  
Increase in allowable stress is not permitted for the design of these components. 
The diameter of the Pad-ears and Trunnions shall be at least 4 times the diameter of the sling/grommet. 
The width of the shaft of Pad-ears and trunnions shall be 1.25 times the diameter of the sling plus 25 mm 
to allow for flattening of the sling. 
Individual cheek plate thickness should preferably not exceed 50 % of the main plate thickness to 
maintain the primacy of the main plate in load transfer to the structure and to provide robustness to lateral 
forces. The individual cheek plate thickness shall in no case exceed 100 % of the main plate thickness. 
The radius of the main plate of pad-eyes shall not be less than 1.5 times the diameter of the pinhole or 
half the pinhole diameter plus 3 inches, whichever is greater. 
 
The pin hole diameter compared with shackle pin diameter shall be as follows: 
 
pin diameter < 50 mm 
hole diameter = pin diameter + 2 mm 
 
pin diameter > 50 mm 
hole diameter ≤ pin diameter + 4 mm 
The Hertz bearing design pressure shall not exceed 2.5 times the minimum yield strength of the contact 
points. 
 
 
Sharp edges are not permitted on the built-up (i.e. non-circular section) shaft of the Pad-ears and 
Trunnions. 
  
In general Pad-eyes shall be preferred over trunnions in accordance with ADNOC Offshore SP-1030 
 
Pad-eyes, Pad-ears and Trunnions shall be examined by NDT and witnessed and certified by Third Party 
prior to lifting. Visual examination of the lifting gear - hooks, slings, grommets, shackles etc. are part of 
this certification. 
  
Structural pad-eyes shall have a valid certificate provided by an ADNOC approved Third Party Lifting 
Equipment Inspector working in approved TPIA in UAE.
